This presentation may contain forward-looking statements, which involve risks and uncertainties as detailed in the disclaimer available in the slides and in the documents filed with the Brazilian Securities Commission, CVM and the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission, SEC. It is a great pleasure to hand the floor to our President and CEO, Francisco Gomes Neto, who will begin the presentation of the company's results. Please, Francisco, go ahead.

Francisco Neto: Thank you, Gui. Good morning and good afternoon, everyone. It is a pleasure to be with you today to discuss Embraer's third quarter '26 results. We delivered record third quarter revenue of $2.3 billion, with revenue and margin expansion across all our businesses. Our backlog reached a new all-time high of $35.7 billion for the eighth consecutive quarter. Based on these results, we are revising up our 2026 revenue and free cash flow guidance. Let me now turn to the key highlights of the quarter. In Commercial Aviation, Farnborough drove 28 firm E2 orders and ANA ordered 8 additional aircraft. In Executive Aviation, we reached a record backlog of $8.2 billion and launched the new Phenom 300EV.

In Defense, Colombia ordered 2 KC-390s while we advanced our partnership with Mahindra, including plans for local assembly line in India. And in Service and Support, we continued expanding our customer agreements. During the quarter, we delivered 22 commercial jets and 41 Executive jets. Total company deliveries increased 6% year-over-year with Commercial Aviation growing 10% and Executive Aviation stable. In Commercial Aviation, we delivered 52 aircraft year-to-date, representing 63% of the midpoint of our full year guidance, 5% points above the 5-year average. In Executive Aviation, we delivered 115 aircraft year-to-date, representing 70% of the midpoint of our full year guidance and a material 10% points above the 5-year average.

These numbers are evidence of our progress in our production leveling initiatives, although we still have room for additional improvement. Our company-wide backlog reached $35.7 billion, up 14% year-over-year and another all-time record for Embraer. All 4 business units increased their backlog year-over-year, led by Defense & Security, up 56% with a strong 2.9 book-to-bill ratio. Executive Aviation backlog grew 12% year-over-year, supported by a 1.4x book-to-bill ratio. Commercial Aviation backlog grew 4% year-over-year, while Services & Support increased 14% with both maintaining book-to-bill ratios above 1x. In addition, we hold approximately $23 billion in options, which could expand our backlog to almost $60 billion if exercised.

With that, I will now hand the call over to Felipe, who will walk you through our financial results. Felipe, the floor is yours.

Felipe Santana de Lima: Thank you, Francisco. Good morning and good afternoon, everyone. I will now talk you through our results by business unit. All comparisons are year-over-year unless otherwise noted. Starting with Commercial Aviation, revenues increased 13% to $696 million, driven by higher volumes and prices. Adjusted EBIT totaled $17 million with a positive 2.4% of margin. The year-over-year improvement was supported by suppliers' compensation. For the fourth quarter, we expect a better customer mix, with full year margins broadly in line with the last year's average. Looking ahead, our ambition is to continue improving Commercial Aviation and profitability to the mid-single-digit level over the next few years.

In Executive Aviation, revenues increased 12% to $655 million, supported by higher prices and product mix. Adjusted EBIT reached $105 million with a positive 16% of margin, circa 130 bps up year-on-year versus a comparison, excluding U.S. tariffs. In Defense & Security, revenues increased 22% to $341 million. Adjusted EBIT was $54 million with a positive 15.7% of margin, driven by higher KC-390 volumes, customer mix and product stage. For the fourth quarter, we do not expect Defense EBIT margins to remain at the strong level seen in the second and third quarters. Still, the division remains on track for another record year, now with profitability around double-digit levels.

Services & Support revenues increased at 30% to $638 million, driven by higher volumes. Adjusted EBIT totaled $108 million with a positive 17% of margin, supported by operating leverage circa 330 bps above year-over-year. At a consolidated level, net revenues increased 17% to $2.35 billion in the third quarter. Revenues were well balanced across our business with Commercial Aviation representing 30%, Executive Aviation, Services more than 25% each and Defense 15%. In the first 9 months, revenues reached $6 billion, representing more than 70% of the midpoint of our full year guidance. Adjusted EBITDA was $370 million in the quarter with a positive 13.5% of margin.

Adjusted EBIT reached $255 million with a positive 10.9% of margin compared to 8.6% a year ago or 9.4% if we exclude U.S. import tariffs. The year-over-year improvement was driven by strong operating performance across all the business. In the first 9 months, adjusted EBIT margin reached 10.7% or 4.2 points above the 5-year historical average. Adjusted free cash flow, excluding Eve, was $628 million in the quarter, reflecting sales-related predelivery payments and stronger operating results. Investment totaled $150 million during the quarter as we continue to invest in new technology and support our current projects and future programs. Adjusted net income was $74 million in the quarter, with a 3.1% of margin compared to 4.2% a year ago.

The year-over-year decrease mainly reflects higher financial expenses from the mark-to-market of our long-term incentive plan and higher taxes. Earnings per ADS now stands at $2.2 on a last 12-month basis. We ended the third quarter with a net cash position. Net cash to adjusted EBITDA, excluding Eve improved to a positive 0.4x from a negative 0.5x a year ago. Our average debt maturity ended the quarter at 8.7 years, while the average cost remained at 5.1%. During the quarter, we declared BRL 154 million in interest on equity. This corresponds to BRL 0.22 per share or circa of $0.17 per ADS.

In 2026, we declared BRL 354 million in interest on equity, representing a dividend yield of around 0.52% based on the share price at the quarter end. Also, for our shareholders, in November, we will change our ADS ratio from 4 common shares per ADS to 1 common share per ADS. This change has no impact on our underlying equity value. Last but not least, a quick word about our guidance. From an operational standpoint, we stand by our commitment to deliver 80 to 85 aircraft in Commercial Aviation and 160 to 170 aircraft in Executive Aviation.

From the financial side, we are happy to increase our revenue guidance to between $8.4 billion to $8.6 billion because of the strong performance of our business. The adjusted EBIT margin remains unchanged at between 10% and 10.6%. However, we raised our adjusted free cash flow guidance once again, now to $700 million or higher, supported by better operational performance and cash generation. With that, I will hand it back to Francisco for his closing remarks. Thank you.

Francisco Neto: Thank you, Felipe. I would like to provide a brief update on Eve's continued progress. The flight test campaign continues to advance with 73 flights completed in more than 3 hours of flight time. Following the completion of hover flights, Eve is now advancing through transition flights with full transition expected before year-end. In summary, we continue to see strong demand across our businesses and positive momentum in our global markets. We would like to welcome Japan Airlines to our E2 family, the second operator of our small narrow-body jet in the country and the Hellenic Air Force to our KC-390 family, the seventh operator of our next-generation military aircraft in Europe.

We remain focused on sales, efficiency and execution while continuing to invest in new technologies for the future. Our progress is driven by the trust of our clients, the support of our suppliers and the commitment, discipline and energy of our people. And we are proud to be certified once again as a great place to work. Together, we remain guided by the pillars of our culture and our motto: Safety first, quality always. With that, we are now ready to take your questions.

We really believe that KC-390 are the best option for the India Air Force (sic) [ Indian Air Force ] because of its characteristics, because it's a more modern product. It has new technologies and their operating costs are highly optimized compared to those of the competition. But as I said, it is ultimately a competition based on price. So it's going to be hard. It's going to be tough, and we are getting ready for that bidding process.

Operator: The next question is also from the chat from Xandu Alves from Jornal do Vale. Francisco, you mentioned Embraer is getting ready for a new product cycle. Could you explain what that means, please? Is it a new family of commercial jets, maybe bigger aircraft? What about in executive and defense? Could you elaborate please?

Francisco Neto: Well, thank you for the question, Xandu. We haven't yet defined what the new product cycle will look like for Embraer. Right now, we are conducting studies. We're assessing the market, talking to clients, talking to suppliers, conducting studies, talking to funding sources, strategic partners so that we can make a decision on how we're going to move forward in the long term, whether we're going to have a new family of executive jets or commercial aircraft, we haven't yet decided on what kind of aircraft or what size yet or if we're going to have a new defense product. That's where Embraer is at the moment.

Operator: The next question is from Bruno Andrade from Veja.

Bruno Andrade: I have a couple of questions. I sent my questions through the chat, but I'll ask them here. What will be the military aircraft front for the next 2 years, '27, '28? Do you see new countries coming into your portfolio? Also, what do you expect in terms of growth for services and support? You mentioned that you wanted to be a relevant line. How can it grow over the next few years? So those are my 2 questions.

Francisco Neto: Thank you for the questions, Bruno. In defense, the KC aircraft line, yes, there is plenty of opportunity and new potential clients for KC-390s. It's a relatively new product, KC-390s went into service in 2019. And since then, we've sold more than 70 aircraft, most of which have been to other countries. Greece has been the latest and the seventh European country to adopt KC-390. We sold it to Asia, to South Korea, to Central Asia, Uzbekistan and South America, we recently announced Colombia. So there are plenty of opportunities for this aircraft globally, including these 2 major projects, India, between 60 and 90 aircraft and the U.S.

Air Force, which is one of the largest clients for that kind of aircraft in the world. So there are plenty of future opportunities for the aircraft in Services & Support, as I mentioned during the presentation. That is one of the major avenues for growth for Embraer because fleets are growing. So the services -- the demand for services and parts also grows. And we also have a new agnostic front, which is engine revisions being conducted by our company in Portugal, OGMA. We have a multibillionaire agreement for GTF engine revisions and the subsidiary is growing a lot.

It was worth USD 230 million, USD 240 million in revenue, and we expect it to get up to $600 million by 2030. So those 2 fronts will be leveraging services, which is a very profitable area for Embraer. Not only in terms of finance but also because it ensures client satisfaction, and it opens the door to new sales for clients.

Bruno Andrade: So you mentioned $600 million by when?

So CEO, Francisco Gomes Neto, Boeing has opened positions for engineers in Brazil, including São José dos Campos, which is where the headquarters for Embraer are. Francisco Neto: Thank you for the question, Marcelo. Actually, the whole industry is growing. Embraer is growing and so is the competition. That's what engineers love, new projects and opportunities to take part in developing new processes, new systems, new aircraft. So we're doing very well, and we are totally prepared for the company's future growth supported by qualified engineers, which are renowned worldwide.

Operator: Next question is also from the chat from João Sorima from O Globo. How are the talks with the U.S. government in terms of KC-390 acquisitions? Any progress?

There are 49 KC-390 suppliers in America. So more than 50% of the content of the aircraft is produced in the U.S. It's a fantastic aircraft for the U.S. Air Force. The first project includes 1 tank (sic) [ tanker ] supplier for jet -- fighter jets.

It's an agile tank (sic) [ tanker ], and it can open the door to KC-390s and it's a great option for the U.S. Air Force for other missions. So yes, we are making progress. But as of yet, nothing has been defined in the short term to buy the aircraft.

Operator: Thank you, Mr. Gomes. Ladies and gentlemen, we now conclude the Q&A session and the earnings release conference call for Embraer. Thank you for joining us, and have a great day.

Canaan Inc. (CAN) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Canaan shifts focus to energy and AI infrastructure as mining demand lags.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Canaan Inc. (CAN -19.15%) reported its results for the quarter ended Sept. 30, 2026, and announced a strategic focus on energy and artificial intelligence high-performance computing (AI HPC) infrastructure. Management reported that the company achieved a milestone for its 1 gigawatt power project in Duval County, Texas, and is advancing a second 1 gigawatt project in Zapata County. The company stated that it is implementing strict capital discipline, reducing research and development spending, and prioritizing shareholder returns through a new share repurchase program. Management indicated that while the bitcoin mining hardware market remains challenged by weak demand and elevated costs, the company intends to utilize its power procurement and site development expertise to participate in larger infrastructure projects.

Operator: Ladies and gentlemen, thank you for standing by and welcome to Canaan, Inc.'s Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Conference Call. [Operator Instructions] Please note that this event is being recorded. Now I'd like to hand the conference over to your speaker today, Gwyn Lauber, Investor Relations for the company. Please go ahead, Gwyn.

Gwyn Lauber: Thank you, operator. Hello, everyone, and welcome to our earnings conference call. Joining us today are our Chairman and CEO, Nangeng Zhang; and our CFO, Jin James Cheng. Leo Wang, Vice President of Capital Markets and Corporate Development; and Xi Zhang, Senior IR Manager, will also be available during the question-and-answer session. Our CEO will start the call by providing an overview of the company and performance highlights for the quarter. Our CFO will then provide details on the company's operating and financial results for the period before we open up the call for your questions. Before we begin, I would like to refer you to our safe harbor statement in our earnings press release.

Today's call will include forward-looking statements. These statements include, but are not limited to, our outlook for the company and statements that estimate or project future operating results and the performance of the company. These statements speak only as of today and the company assumes no obligation to revise any forward-looking statements that may be made in today's press release, call or webcast except as required by law. These statements do not guarantee future performance and are subject to risks, uncertainties and assumptions.

Please refer to the press release and the risk factors and documents we file with the Securities and Exchange Commission, including our most recent annual report on Form 20-F for information on risks, uncertainties and assumptions that may cause actual results to differ materially from those set forth in such statements. In addition, during today's call, we will discuss both GAAP financial measures and certain non-GAAP financial measures, which we believe are useful as supplemental measures of the company's performance. These non-GAAP measures should be considered in addition to and not as a substitute for or in isolation from GAAP results.

You can find additional disclosures regarding these non-GAAP measures, including reconciliations with comparable GAAP results, in our earnings press release, which is posted on the company's website. With that, I will now turn the call over to our Chairman and CEO, Nangeng Zhang. NG , please go ahead.

Nangeng Zhang: Thank you, Gwyn. Hello, everyone. This is NG. Thank you for joining in today's earnings call. Over the past few quarters, we have discussed Canaan's strategic transformation on several occasions. Today, I would like to begin with the changes taking place in our industry, then explain how we are adapting our operations and share the latest progress in our transformation. First, the bitcoin mining hardware industry is undergoing profound structural changes. Demand remains weak, industry inventories are elevated and hardware selling prices remain under pressure. At the same time, rapidly growing AI demand is absorbing significant advanced node chip manufacturing capacity, driving wafer fabrication costs sharply higher and tightening supply.

As a result, even higher performing next-generation mining machines may be difficult to produce at a reasonable cost and generate adequate commercial returns. We expect these factors to constrain the production and deployment of new mining machines over the next few years, making it difficult for bitcoin network hash rate to sustain its fast pace of growth. For operators with low-cost power and existing equipment, this could improve long-term mining economics. In response, Canaan is adjusting its approach to R&D, manufacturing and capital investment. Over the past 2 quarters, several of our next-generation mining ASIC development projects have been completed or are substantially complete.

We have received some engineering samples with test results meeting our design expectations and additional samples continue to arrive. We believe these technological advances are sufficient to meet market needs over the next several years. This enables us to significantly reduce ongoing R&D spending on new products while retaining the ability to begin mass production when commercial conditions are appropriate. We will continue to pursue economically attractive opportunities in mining hardware sales and bitcoin mining, but we will no longer rely on sustained large-scale investments and successive product generations to drive the company's growth. Canaan's long-term focus is shifting towards energy and AI HPC computing infrastructure. This transformation rests on a clear business foundation.

Through years of bitcoin mining operations in North America, we have developed expertise in power procurement, site development, equipment deployment and operations management while building long-term relationships with utilities, local communities and partners. Since 2025, we have deliberately applied these capabilities to developing power resources on a large scale. As demand for AI infrastructure grows rapidly, land and infrastructure with reliable access to power are becoming critical to the industry's development. Today, I'm pleased to share an important milestone. Our 1 gigawatt large-load project in Duval County, Texas, recently received a conditional Studied Load - Firm classification under ERCOT's Batch Zero process.

This achievement reflects our team's sustained development efforts and investment and marks a substantive step forward in the grid interconnection process. We are continuing to advance the remaining grid interconnection procedures. On the commercial development side, we are engaging in deep discussions with a U.S. institution with an investment-grade credit profile regarding a potential strategic partnership. We believe partners with strong capital resources, credit support and the ability to deliver large infrastructure projects can help Canaan participate more efficiently in major energy infrastructure developments. This is particularly important for a company of our size.

By drawing on our expertise, we can play a meaningful role in the value chain of large projects while controlling our capital commitment and risk and earning returns with our contribution. We aim to build this collaborative development model into a sustainable business over the long term. The significance of this progress extends well beyond a single project. AI data center infrastructure represents a substantial market opportunity; but it also requires significant capital, specialized expertise and long development and construction time lines. Bitcoin mining as another form of high-density computing application offers greater flexibility. It can help the grid balance peak and off-peak power demand and provide additional value to local communities through heat recovery and other uses.

Over the long term, Canaan believes power resources can serve different purpose. For projects suited to AI HPC, we will prioritize the corresponding development and partnership opportunities. For lower-cost power resources that are better suited to bitcoin mining, we are also open to prioritize in bitcoin mining. We will assess the economics and development conditions of each project and the flexibility to select the business model that creates the greatest value. In addition, Canaan has historically adhered to our philosophy of developing jointly with partners across the ecosystem. We believe this approach remains equally applicable to today's AI HPC development project and can help the company go further and more steadily.

At the same time, we are advancing another 1 gigawatt power resources project in Zapata County, Texas. This project is in early stage development with preparations for grid studies underway. The milestone in Duval and our continued process in Zapata demonstrate that we are gradually building both a pipeline of power resources projects and ongoing development capability in North America. We aim to translate the industry relationships and execution experience we have accumulated over the years into an infrastructure business that creates sustained value. Finally, let me turn to capital discipline. We are taking firm action to control operating expenses. further reduce administrative costs and R&D spending and strictly manage new production commitments and capital investment.

For large infrastructure projects, we will prioritize structures that draw on our expertise, bringing in partner capital and allocated risk appropriately. Our goal is to use less of our own capital to participate in higher value projects while gradually improving shareholder value and capital efficiency. I want to emphasize that Canaan's transformation goes beyond the shift from bitcoin mining to AI HPC data centers. More fundamentally, we are shifting our focus from capital expansion to generating returns on capital and ultimately enhancing shareholder value. This requires us to apply strict capital discipline to our treasury policy, R&D spending and capital investment planning; allocating capital only to opportunities that can clearly create shareholder value at a manageable level of risk.

We must be equally disciplined about taking on debt or diluting shareholders. We will pursue growing only when it compounds shareholder value and strengthens our appeal to institutional investors. The operating environment remains challenging, but Canaan's transformation is moving from strategy to execution. Building on years of technological development, we have completed a major phase of R&D and begun to make tangible progress in energy infrastructure. Our business priorities and path forward are becoming clearer and we are -- I am confident in this direction. We will remain focused on project execution, capital efficiency and commercial returns, demonstrating the value of this transformation through sustained operating results. Thank you, everyone.

Now I will turn the call over to James to review our quarter 3 financial results.

James Cheng: Thank you, NG, and hello, everyone. This is James speaking in our Singapore headquarters. Just now, NG described the 2 shifts underway at Canaan. First is from bitcoin mining toward energy and AI HPC infrastructure and more fundamentally, from capital expansion toward capital returns and shareholder value. My remarks today will show how both are reflected in our numbers this quarter. First, the capital we have committed to our energy projects, then our treasury and shareholder actions and finally, our third quarter results and outlook. The clearest example this quarter is our Duval County project. As of September 30, we held $50 million in cash collateral for a letter of credit in relation to power projects.

This is a concrete commitment of capital behind the milestone NG just described. The funds remain on our balance sheet as restricted cash. Cash and cash equivalents were $22.8 million as of September 30. The decline from the end of June mainly reflected the funds we moved into the collateral account. Including the $50 million collateral, our total cash position was $72.8 million, up 10.3% from the end of June. Beyond the collateral, we had approximately $3.3 million in project prepayments as of September 30 related to our infrastructure plans. In October, we also completed an approximately $1.1 million power-related land purchase for our 1 gigawatt power resource project in Zapata County, Texas.

Securing suitable land is one of the core development capabilities. This piece of land gives us a base for application for power interconnection and reflects our commitment to building a lasting presence in the United States. For large infrastructure projects, we intend to bring in capital from partners and minimize development uncertainty so that we can participate in high-value projects with less of our own capital. The same discipline guides how we manage our treasury. NG also emphasized discipline in our treasury policy. During the third quarter, we monetized our Ethereum holdings and a portion of our bitcoin holdings generating approximately $40 million in cash. The average selling prices were approximately $79,000 per bitcoin and $2,400 per Ethereum.

Of these proceeds, we used $5.4 million to repurchase 13.6 million ADSs. We regularly review our treasury strategy weighing the potential returns from holding digital assets, investing in our businesses and repurchasing our shares. We believe our shares are undervalued relative to our long-term value. So we prepared to allocate a portion of our capital to buybacks. We believe our current share price does not fully reflect our long-term value and share repurchases are an important capital allocation priority for us. Our Board has approved a new share repurchase authorization of up to $100 million over the next 12 months, under which we are launching an initial program of up to $20 million.

As our available funds grow, we intend to pursue repurchases more actively. The pace and scale of repurchases will depend on our financial position, market conditions and applicable regulations. We are also planning an ADS ratio change. The change is designed to preserve the overall economic interest represented by investors' ADS holdings. By increasing the per ADS trading price, we believe it could make our ADSs more accessible to institutional investors whose investment policies limit holdings of lower priced securities. As our business increasingly centers on energy and computing infrastructure where progress is measured over quarters rather than months, we have discontinued our stand-alone monthly mining report. Key mining metrics will be included in our quarterly results.

We will continue to announce material developments separately when timely disclosure is required under applicable rules. During the third quarter, we appointed KPMG in Singapore as our auditor succeeding KPMG in Beijing. I would like to thank the KPMG Beijing team for their professionalism and support and welcome the KPMG Singapore team. The change aligns our audit arrangements more closely with our increasingly international operations. Now let me turn to our third quarter results. For the third quarter, we reported revenue of $14.5 million within our guidance range of $11 million to $15 million.

Net income was $8.0 million driven primarily by $51.9 million in fair value gains on digital assets and related financial derivatives reflecting the recovery in bitcoin prices during the quarter. Adjusted EBITDA was $22.4 million and also benefited from these gains. If bitcoin prices continue to trend upward, the appreciation of our accumulated digital assets could further contribute to our profitability. This aligns to our digital asset treasury strategy. Our mining gross margin improved significantly to close to 30% in the third quarter, up from about 20% in the second quarter. We generated 133 bitcoins during the quarter. Even after monetizing a portion of our holdings, we held approximately 1,875 bitcoins at September 30.

By maintaining a substantial bitcoin treasury through the downturn, we were able to participate in the subsequent price recovery and the increase in the value of these holdings made an important contribution to our results this quarter. Total operating expenses were $34.2 million, down 15.5% from the prior quarter. As NG mentioned, several of our next-generation mining ASIC projects have been completed or are nearly complete and we believe these results can support market demand over the coming years. As a result, we no longer need to heavily rely on continuous large-scale R&D investments to drive growth, which allows us to continue managing our operating expenses prudently.

Our inventory declined to $119 million on September 30, marking its lowest level in the past 5 quarters. Given the demand environment and rising wafer costs, we are carefully managing new production commitments and aligning purchases with actual customer demand. For Project ABC, we recognized $1.6 million in equity investment income during the quarter. This gain was noncash and included acquisition-related accounting adjustments. In cash terms, we received $1.9 million from ABC comprising $1.2 million in distributions and $0.7 million in sales proceeds. For us, these cash receipts are the meaningful measure. Our focus remains on ABC's operating performance and its ability to generate real cash returns over time.

Beyond our own mining operations, we are also expanding the applications of our mining equipment to help customers get the most value from every megawatt used. In Northern Europe, an 8-megawatt district heating project repurposes waste heat from liquid cooled computing equipment to provide heating for approximately 2,800 households. We are also exploring greenhouse heating in Canada, home heating through Avalon Home and using associated gas from oil fields that would otherwise be flared. In summary, this quarter is a profitable quarter with committed capital to our energy strategy, return of capital to shareholders and tight control over costs, expense and inventory. As NG said, our transformation is moving from planning to execution.

On the financial side, we will continue measuring the progress by capital efficiency and returns. We will remain disciplined in our use of capital and pursue growth only where it compounds value per share. Turning to our outlook. We expect total revenue for the fourth quarter of 2026 to be between $18 million and $22 million. This outlook reflects our current assessment of the market and the operating conditions. Actual results may vary with changes in bitcoin prices, industry demand and other market conditions. Thank you. Operator, we are now ready to take questions.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] Your question comes from Kevin Dede from JonesTrading.

Sky Moore: This is Sky Moore calling in for Kevin Dede. My first one is with the next ASIC team largely complete, what specific market conditions would lead you to start mass production? Where should R&D spending settle and how do you plan to work down the remaining inventory?

Nangeng Zhang: I think over the past 2 quarters, we have completed or substantially completed several next-generation mining ASIC development. And during this quarter, we have received some of the engineering samples and we have tested them and the test results meet our design expectations and additional samples are arriving. So I think this technology portfolio can support the market demand over the next several years. Our next step is to determine when commercial conditions is justified bringing these technologies into volume production. Advanced node wafer costs have risen significantly while mining equipment demand remains very weak. So we will, therefore, consider customer demand, production cost and expected returns when setting the production schedule.

So I think this technology portfolio allow us to substantially reduce ongoing mining ASIC R&D spending while retaining the ability to launch new products where appropriate. So that is consistent with our broader transition, continue to pursue economically attractive mining equipment opportunities while directing more resources towards energy and AI HPC infrastructure.

Sky Moore: Great. My next question would be with most of the bitcoin pledged against loans and unrestricted cash floating around about $23 million, how do you guys plan to fund that $20 million buyback alongside the Duval buildout and what would lead you towards the full $100 million?

James Cheng: I think we believe current share price does not fully reflect the company's long-term value. So buybacks are a very important capital allocation priority for the company. After providing normal operations and also committed project funding needs, we would like to become more active in doing repurchasing shares. I think we have already taken action in the third quarter. We generated approximately $14 million in cash from monetizing Ethereum and a portion of our bitcoin holdings and used already $5.4 million of those proceeds to repurchase shares. So recently our Board approved a new program of up to $100 million over the next 12 months.

So we started from a $20 million program and I think our direction is very clear. As long as we continue to see the attractive value in the shares, buybacks will be one of the priority uses of additional available funds. I think the timing and the size of repurchase will reflect our funding position, market conditions and applicable regulatory requirements. So we will take pace and we will consider the market situation and step-by-step do this stock repurchase program. Hope that answers your question.

Operator: Your next question comes from Ben Sommers from U.S. Bancorp BTIG.

Benjamin Sommers: So I wanted to ask on the Duval County site. Just wanted to ask a bit more on the discussions you're having with a potential partner there. Would this be a potential partnership where you could give up some equity in the site? Would the partner kind of handle the development process? Just how do you think about what you want from that strategic partner?

Nangeng Zhang: Yes. We are considered by confidentiality agreements and are not in position to disclose further details at this time. But our objective is to generate positive cash flow from these projects while would support the share repurchase and provide funding for subsequent such power resources development, land acquisition and related activities.

Benjamin Sommers: Super helpful. And then switching over, talking about the heating applications of the ASICs. Can you just talk a bit about how that is being incorporated into the development pipeline here as we also manage the inventory and try to optimize the balance sheet?

Nangeng Zhang: Let me see. Yes, we have a corporate in North Europe. Currently, we already deployed several megawatts of our equipment generating over 80 degrees Celsius for about 2,800 families for the heating and now it already generates USD 1.1 million in revenue. It is about 8 megawatts hydro cooled mining equipment project we already delivered and in operation. It uses recovered mining heat to serve about 2,800 households. These applications create value from both mining and heating and remain an area we are developing. Still we have ongoing contracts in negotiation and I think the scale of the heating program will scale up.

Operator: Your next question comes from Kevin Cassidy from Rosenblatt Securities.

Kevin Cassidy: The midterm elections are top of mind for investors. And I just want to understand how Canaan is set up for the Clarity Act and it doesn't look like that would pass and whether you have -- there's a plan B on digital assets.

Nangeng Zhang: I think overall we expect the impact to be limited. The Clarity Act mainly concerns regulatory framework for digital assets market and our existing operations do not depend on its passage. And also, our primary focus is shifting towards energy and AI HPC infrastructure where its main drivers are power resources, project economics and customer demand. We will continue to monitor policy developments, but they do not change our current strategic direction.

Kevin Cassidy: Okay. And maybe as long as you're mentioning energy and you have these facilities in Texas, are you expecting any issues with the change in the government in Texas, the governor?

Nangeng Zhang: I think currently there's so many news comes out in this week. So now I think we are looking closely to any kind of changes in the policy especially in Texas. But personally, I think the energy infrastructure is quite useful and I think it's good for America. So our focus is on the early-stage energy infrastructure development. We are not building -- currently we are not focusing on like building big AI HPC data centers also. So we expect to earn returns that reflect our contribution while keeping the capital requirements and risks manageable.

Operator: Your next question comes from Nick Giles from B. Riley Securities.

Nick Giles: Good to see some continued progress at these Texas sites. I just was curious if you could outline some of the next steps before the classification becomes final at Duval and how we should think about the ultimate timing of energization there and how much power you think would be available initially?

Nangeng Zhang: Yes. I think we are advancing 2 1-gigawatt power projects in Texas. The Duval County project has received a conditional Studied Load - Firm classification in the ERCOT's Batch Zero process and we are progressing the subsequent grid procedures. And the Zapata County is in early stage development and the preparation for grid studies. That means it will be a few months later than Duval. So for timing, we encourage investors to follow ERCOT announcements for the next stage of grid studies and capacity allocation as well. We are currently looking towards 2027 when the specific schedule subject to ERCOT's subsequent update.

Nick Giles: Okay. Makes sense. Maybe just next with bitcoin up about 30% since the end of the second quarter, what kind of visibility do you have on demand as we look to 2027? And then what's driving the step-up in revenue in the fourth quarter?

Nangeng Zhang: To be honest, we do not have yet seen a meaningful recovery in mining equipment demand. Higher bitcoin prices have not translated sufficiently into new equipment orders. Customers remain very focused on payback periods and the current mining returns and the equipment price are not yet attractive enough to support large-scale new purchases. So we remain cautious about conventional mining equipment demand in the fourth quarter and we'll schedule production and deliveries against actual orders. Our current Q4 total revenue guidance is USD 18 million to USD 22 million. That includes mining equipment sales and other business revenue. So it should not be interpreted as evidence that demand for new machines has recovered.

Operator: Your question comes from Ian Generous from Compass Point Research & Trading.

Ian Generous: This is Ian Generous calling in for Michael Donovan. So firstly, just looking on inventory. How Canaan is positioned today, including finished miners, chips and wafers on hand and then any outstanding purchase commitments?

Nangeng Zhang: Let me see. I think by the end of third quarter, the carrying value of our inventory was approximately USD 8.2 million, down significantly from USD 25.3 million in second quarter. The details of our purchase commitments are commercially confidential so we cannot disclose the specific amount. And some of the wafers are secured earlier with price relatively low and we are deferring delivery with our suppliers' support. I think further write-down remains a risk, but the launch of next-generation product is not the only consideration. The value of existing inventory also depends on whether new machines can be produced at scale to competitive cost, actual market selling prices and customer demand.

With wafer costs now substantially higher, even a more energy-efficient next-generation product may not be able to reach the market and scale at sufficiently low price. We will continue to assess inventory value and record any necessary write-downs or provisions as appropriate. At the same time, we will align production with orders and control new purchase commitments and inventory risk.

Ian Generous: That's helpful. And then looking at the demand for mining rigs across North America and the rest of the world, our customers, do you see are they pausing orders and how are they financing their purchases?

Nangeng Zhang: Okay. I will say bit more about this. I think weak demand for mining equipment is a global issue not only North America driven primarily by inadequate investment returns. I think we can do a simple calculation can illustrate this challenge. At current mining revenue of approximately USD 0.023 per terahash per day and in average the electricity cost is about $0.015 per terahash per day. So the gross margin is only about $0.017 remaining after electricity. So assuming revenue and the electricity costs stay constant and before other operating and financing costs, the equipment price would have be about $6 per terahash.

Based on our understanding of industry costs, that is well below the cost of any existing industry mining equipment inventories across the industry. So producing new machines at this much, much higher wafer prices would widen the gap between production cost and the prices customers can justify paying. AI is also competing for power and sites, making low-cost mining locations harder to secure in some regions and putting upward pressure on electricity prices. Even customers who can obtain financing may therefore struggling to put the deployment opportunities that offer reasonable returns. So you can see the trend in network hash rate. The total network hash rate is a constant with this assessment.

Hash rate has declined overall from its 2025 peak with a period of recovery, but it has not returned to that height. Also based on our market observations, mining equipment used for electronic heating in Mainland China has been switched back on as winter approaches so contributing a seasonal increase in hash rate. This primarily reflects existing equipment retaining into operation rather than new purchases. So taken together; net hash rate, hash price and equipment selling price; indicate to us that the market for new machines are still contracting. So we are not optimistic about a near-term recovery in demand.

Operator: I'd like to turn the call back over to the company for any closing remarks.

Gwyn Lauber: Thank you, operator, and thanks, everyone, for joining us today. If you have any additional questions, feel free to reach out to us. And we look forward to speaking with you throughout the quarter. Thanks very much.

Operator: This concludes today's conference call. Thank you for participating. You may now disconnect.

Inside Danielle Lauder’s Barbie Dream Closet

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Designed by AMMOR Architecture, the ultra-pink, mega dressing room is a cinematic fantasy come to life.

Cher Horowitz had a touch-screen conveyer belt in her color-coded closet. Carrie Bradshaw kept her Manolos in an unusually spacious high-fashion-filled walk-in. And Margot Robbie’s Barbie displayed her hot-pink wardrobe like doll’s packaging in her wall-free fantasy villa. Danielle Lauder, the great-granddaughter of Estée Lauder, created her own iteration of the dream closet—this time, in real life.

Her own inspiration? “I always thought of Belle in Beauty and the Beast in the library, just rolling around on her ladder,” she says. “I’m not much of a reader, but as a collector, I knew that’s what I wanted.”

Her wish list for the jewel-box space also included curving walls, glossy pink lacquer, illuminated marble, and a capsule-shaped vanity inspired by the makeup collection she designed for Estée Lauder in 2019. The beauty empire was founded by—and named after—her great-grandmother in 1946; and her father, William P. Lauder, serves as the chairman of the board. (Danielle now oversees her own production company, Act 4 Artists.)

To make her dream a reality, Lauder turned to architects Goil Amornvivat and Thomas Morbitzer, founders of New York City–based AMMOR Architecture. The duo had instant chemistry with Lauder, who was especially taken with the meticulous diorama they created for her.

“Tom and I both love Barbie,” says Amornvivat. “So we tweaked our design process to make an architectural model that was more like a dollhouse, where you could switch out surfaces.” Their efforts worked on Lauder, herself a Barbie fan, and the pair walked away with the commission to not just create the dressing room but to overhaul the entire industrial-style apartment in the West Village of Manhattan, which was then being used as an art studio.

“They both really understood and saw my vision,” says Lauder. “Plus, they were the most excited, which made me more excited. Goil is like the Energizer Bunny.”

The 250-square-foot dressing room is, Lauder says, “the biggest room in the apartment besides the living room.” It’s also where Lauder spends the most time. She enters just off the primary bedroom via an arched passageway finished in custom bronze plaster, emerging from the dark portal into a pink fantasia where her expansive fashion collection is arrayed like a personal boutique. “The idea was for it to be part display area but also a functional closet,” she says.

At the center is a custom pink vanity outfitted with fluted drawers, dual-sided mirrors, and a top made of richly veined Midnight Rose marble, sourced from New York Stone. Lauder keeps her collection of vintage and reissued Estée Lauder compacts in the drawers. “I love collecting them and having them out on display or in a nice drawer,” says Lauder. “They’re beautiful little artifacts.”

On the curving walls, rounded niches display shelving and racks against illuminated stone, part of Lauder’s request for layered lighting. “I love the backlit marble,” she says. “It’s so flattering.” She keeps her enviable bag and shoe collection on permanent display, maintained with help from her housekeeper. Lauder draws from the selection on a daily basis, with particular affection for family heirlooms such as her mom’s vintage Dolce & Gabbana handbags.

Overhead, Amornvivat and Morbitzer devised a futuristic lighting installation, stretching mesh taut over an oval-shaped light source to create a seamless surface. “The light in there is controllable by color, and it's dimmable,” notes Morbitzer. “It’s kind of like a skylight, which was important because it’s the only room without windows,” adds Amornvivat.

The bespoke elements continue: heated floors, an ice maker, a perfume bar, plenty of drawers, and a pair of walk-in closets cleverly concealed behind matching lacquered doors. “The whole room is custom millwork,” says Amornvivat.

And Lauder did get the sliding ladder of her dreams. “That was my hill to die on,” she says. “I’ve always struggled to reach things in my closet, so it was definitely practical. And my grandfather had one in his apartment in the library when I was growing up, and I just loved it.”

Now that the closet is complete, Lauder has a hard time leaving. “I spend more time in here than I do probably even sleeping in bed,” she says with a laugh. The space is remarkably faithful to the original idea that Amornvivat and Morbitzer presented in their dollhouse diorama. “I wanted it to be totally mine, feminine and fun, and still very warm and inviting,” she says. “It’s become my ultimate bachelorette hub.”

Geoffrey Montes is an associate editor at ELLE Decor with a serious love for all things real estate and design. Before that, he worked at Architectural Digest, Galerie, and Preservation magazines, covering everything from jaw-dropping listings to world-famous architects and design events like Salone del Mobile and Homo Faber.

CleanupBuddy 2.0 expands beyond the keyboard with new display cleaning features

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Developer Gui Rambo updated CleanupBuddy today, expanding its ability to prevent accidental inputs while cleaning your Mac from the keyboard to the touchscreen. Here are the details.

If you’re not familiar with CleanupBuddy, this is a nice little app that locks the keyboard to prevent accidental inputs while the user cleans it up.

Once they’re done cleaning, users can press and hold both Cmd keys to unlock the keyboard, then proceed to grease it up again.

The app also features a fun little animated character that reacts to your cleaning sessions with playful (and sometimes snarky) comments.

Today, CleanupBuddy was updated to version 2.0, with options to dim the display and ignore touch inputs, making it useful for users of both the new touchscreen MacBook Pro and Macs with traditional displays.

You can use the new “Lock Touch Screen” option to block touch input while cleaning a supported touch screen. On Macs without a touch screen, the “Dim Display” option can still be used to darken the display for easier cleaning.

Today’s update also tweaks the in-app character, which now uses on-device Apple Intelligence to generate personalized comments before and after cleaning sessions, based on stats such as time spent cleaning, key presses, and mouse clicks.

Rambo explains that these stats are collected only during cleaning sessions and stored locally on the Mac, and that users can independently turn off both cleanup stats and Apple Intelligence integration in the app’s settings.

Other tweaks in today’s update include temporarily disabling Sticky Keys and its Shift-key shortcut during cleaning sessions, as well as improving the readability of the “Start Cleanup” button when using Liquid Glass.

CleanupBuddy 2.0 is available for $2.99 and is compatible with macOS 14 and later, while the new personalized messages require macOS 26 or later, with Apple Intelligence enabled and its models ready to use.

10 Greatest Disney Movies That Are 10/10 Masterpieces

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

In 1923, brothers Walt and Roy O. Disney founded the Walt Disney Company to create their own run of animated shorts. From such humble beginnings, Disney has now grown to become perhaps the largest media conglomerate in the world, having absorbed the likes of Lucasfilm, Marvel, and 20th Century Fox. Though they now have their hands in everything from live action movies to theme parks, they still put effort into their animated movies, though recent years have seen them eclipsed by studios like DreamWorks and Sony when it comes to creativity and effort.

Still, there's no denying Disney has created some of the most well-known and beloved movies of all time. Regardless of when they first released, these movies continue to delight new and old viewers thanks to their strong writing, iconic characters, catchy songs, and the sense of fun and classic "Disney Magic" that ties them all together.

Aladdin (Scott Weinger) is a street rat living in the city of Agrabah with his monkey Abu (Frank Welker), who longs for a life of wealth and luxury so that nobody will ever look down on him again. After he is arrested following a chance meeting with the rebellious Princess Jasmine (Linda Larkin), Aladdin is recruited by the Royal Vizier, Jafar (Jonathan Freeman), to go inside the Cave of Wonders (Frank Welker) and retrieve a magic lamp. Aladdin discovers that the lamp contains a genie (Robin Williams) who is bound to grant three wishes to whomever holds the lamp, which he plans to use to become a prince and woo Jasmine.

Aladdin is one of the best examples of Disney's ability to combine comedy with heartfelt storytelling. Williams' performance as the Genie is so iconic that it changed how animated films handle celebrity voice actors, but when not throwing out rapid-fire jokes, he has a very sincere relationship with Aladdin that plays into the movie's themes about self-acceptance and integrity. The rest of the film is also great too: Jaffar stands tall as one of Disney's best villains thanks to his charisma, the animation is colorful and stylistic, and the songs catchy and all key to the story and characters.

While sailing from England to Port Royal, Elizabeth Turner (Lucinda Dryzek and Kiera Knightly) helps to rescue a young boy named William Turner (Dylan Smith and Orlando Bloom) from a shipwreck, and takes his golden medallion so he wouldn't be mistaken for a pirate. Years later, Elizabeth is kidnapped by the dreaded pirate ship The Black Pearl, and learns that the medallion is the key to freeing them from their curse of undeath. To save her, Will recruits the help of Captain Jack Sparrow (Johnny Depp), an eccentric pirate who has a history with the Pearl and its captain, Hector Barbossa (Geoffrey Rush).

Pirates of the Caribbean: The Curse of the Black Pearl is the most successful live-action adaptation of Disney’s theme park rides, and spawned an entire franchise of swashbuckling adventure stories. The movie is the perfect combination of memorable characters, fast-paced sword fights, high-seas action, and creative storytelling that weaves mythology and real history. Of all the characters, Jack Sparrow easily stands out the most thanks to Depp's phenomenal performance, and has cemented himself as one of the most iconic pirates in pop-culture history.

Ariel (Jodi Benson) is the youngest daughter of the Sea King, Triton (Kenneth Mars), but, much to his frustration, she has a fascination with the surface world and regularly goes looking for human artifacts. One night, she rescues Prince Eric (Christopher Daniel Barnes) after his ship is caught in a storm, and falls deeply in love with him. The whole affair is overseen by the sea witch Ursula (Pat Carroll), who plans to use Ariel's emotional vulnerability in her plan to usurp control of the sea from Triton.

The Little Mermaid is the movie that kick-started Disney's Renaissance, an era where Disney became the undisputed king of pop-culture. Even today, the movie still captivates audiences with its Broadway-style musical numbers by Alan Menken and Howard Ashman, and the underwater world is so lavishly animated. Character wise, Ariel and Eric have a pretty solid romance thanks to the film giving them time to develop as a couple, Triton is a solid father who goes through an arch regarding letting his daughter live her own life, and Urusla is so deliciously evil with her showmanship and Carroll's bombastic voice.

When an evil queen (Lucille La Verne) learns that her stepdaughter, Snow White (Adriana Caselotti), is more beautiful than her, she tasks her huntsman (Stuart Buchanan) with killing her. The huntsman can't bring himself to do it and instead tells the princess to run into the woods and never return. With the help of woodland animals, Snow White arrives at a cottage inhabited by seven dwarfs, who agree to let her stay with them.

Snow White and the Seven Dwarfs is not just the first Disney film, but also the first animated feature film in North America, and the first cell-shaded animated feature film. The movie set the bar for how to do fairy tale adaptations, with a story that plays on the audience's emotions to pull them into the character's struggles, hopes, and ambitions. The queen also set a very high bar for Disney villains thanks to her majestic and commanding presence in her normal form, and the cackling, bombastic villainy she exews upon transforming into her grotesque hag form.

Deep in the woods on a spring's day, the son of a stag called the Great Prince of the Forest (Fred Shields) is born. Named Bambi (Bobby Stewart, Hardie Albright, John Sutherland, and Donnie Duunagan), he grows up in the care of his mother (Paula Winslowe) and makes friends with a rabbit named Thumper (Peter Behn, Tim Davis, and Sam Edwards) and a skunk nicknamed Flower (Stan Alexander, Tim Davis, and Sterling Holloway). However, things become more difficult as the seasons change, and a mysterious creature called Man comes to the forest.

Bambi has gone from being a financial failure on release to one of the most beloved and respected animated animal films. It avoids excessive anthropomorphization of its characters, which, combined with the more realistic animal designs, presents the movie more as a snapshot of the natural world than a proper narrative. Its backgrounds by Tyrus Wong are also some of the most stylistic in any Disney film, using watercolor patterns to create a forest that feels all the more realistic and mysterious.

Four of the largest communities on the planet, and four completely different reasons to belong to one. Here is yours, what it would give you, and the argument you would be having within a week.

You belong to the fandom that is really about comfort. Hogwarts works on people because it is a place rather than a plot, and the pull is not the wizard war so much as the corridors, the food, the weather and the sense that somebody is expecting you.

You belong to the deepest fandom on this list, the one where somebody has always read further than you and is quietly delighted to say so. Middle-earth rewards patience more than any other world in popular culture.

You belong to the loudest and largest tent of the four, the one where three generations are all watching the same thing and none of them agree about any of it. That disagreement is not a flaw in this fandom, it is the main activity.

You belong to the thinking fandom, the one that turns up for the argument rather than the explosion. Trek has always been a series of ethical problems in space, and the people who love it love being asked what they would have done.

In an alternate 1940s where humans live alongside living cartoon characters, Eddie Valiant (Bob Hoskins) is a private detective who has fallen onto hard times after the death of his brother at the hands of a toon. The day after he photographs Jessica Rabbit (Kathleen Turner) playing patty cake with the owner of Toontown, Marvin Acme (Stubby Kaye), he learns that Jessica's husband, Roger (Charles Fleischer), apparently murdered Acme in a jealous rage. Roger, however, seeks out Eddie to prove his innocence and protect him from the psychotic judge of Toontown, Judge Doom (Christopher Lloyd).

Who Framed Roger Rabbit is a revolutionary movie that has some of the best examples of mixing animated characters into live-action footage. This is thanks to the incredible level of detail that went into their creation, from using strings to move items the toon characters would hold to actively changing the shading on the characters to reflect changes in lighting. Beyond its technical achievements, it's also a very solid mystery with a strong film noir aesthetic, and some terrific performances from Hoskins and Lloyd.

To repair Mickey Mouse's declining popularity, Walt conceived an animated short based on Paul Dukas' symphonic poem The Sorcerer's Apprentice. As the budget grew too big to justify, Walt decided to turn the project into a feature film that would combine multiple shorts based on classical music. Leopold Stokowski was brought on as conductor, and Deems Taylor served as Master of Ceremonies.

The result was Fantasia, an ambitious film that combined music and animation like never before or since. The Disney animators put their all into each segment, matching the music with everything from proper narratives to simple abstract shapes that might come to one's mind as they hear the music. Disney would try to make similar films, but none of them ever matched the artistic creativity of the original movie.

Frustrated that his children Jane (Karen Dotrice) and Michael (Matthew Garber) have driven off another nanny, George Banks (David Tomlinson) places an ad in the paper for a strict nanny who will set them right. The next day, a wind blows away all the women who came to apply, and George instead hires Mary Poppins (Dame Julie Andrews), a woman who flew in on an umbrella, and meets the requirements of a separate ad made by the kids. Mary is indeed stern with the kids as needed, but also takes them on fantastical adventures with the help of her handyman friend, Bert (Dick Van Dyke).

Mary Poppins was famously despised by the author of the original books, P. L. Travers, but to general audience, it is one of Disney's biggest successes. This is thanks to its ability to blend the fun, whimsical adventures that Mary Poppins takes the children through with the hard-hitting but relatable reality of a father trying to rebuild a relationship with his kids. The songs by The Sherman Brothers help to convey this wide spectrum of emotions, with one song in particular, "Feed the Birds," having the honor of being Walt Disney's favourite from any of his movies.

All the animals of the Pride Lands travel to Pride Rock to celebrate the birth of the lion cub Simba (Johnathan Taylor Thomas and Matthew Broderick), son of King Mufasa (James Earl Jones). As he grows, Simba is educated by his father about the importance of maintaining the circle of life, but the young cub is too focused on how exciting it will be when he has the throne. Unfortunately, his uncle Scar (Jeremy Irons) lusts after the throne himself, and makes an alliance with a clan of hyenas to eliminate both Simba and Mufasa so he can take it.

The Lion King began life as a side-project to fill a 1994 release slot while Disney's best talent worked on Pocahontas, but it has since gone down in history as one of the most beloved animated films, and the highest grossing traditionally-animated one. The movie uses bold colors, wide shots, and foreground tricks to make the African Savannah feel massive and epic in scale, which pairs well with the story's themes about duty, responsibility, and learning from the mistakes of the past. Scar is also widely regarded as one of the best Disney villains thanks to how seamlessly the animation by Andreas Deja mixes with Irons' performance, creating a cunning, vicious villain who actually gets further in his plans than most Disney antagonists.

After refusing to shelter an old woman during a storm, a spoiled prince (Robby Benson) is turned into a beast and told to find love and be loved in return before the last petal of an enchanted rose falls, or he and his staff shall be cursed forever. Ten years later, he imprisons an inventor named Maurice (Rex Everhart) who trespassed during a storm, and meets Maurice's daughter, Belle (Paige O'Hara), when she comes looking for him. Belle offers to take her father's place as the Beast's prisoner, and, though things don't start off the best, the two do eventually come to see that there is more to the other than first appears.

Beauty and the Beast overcame a brutal production to not only receive near-universal acclaim, but also become the first animated film to be nominated for the Academy Award for Best Picture—and remains the only 2D animated film and the only one from when the Oscars had five nominees instead of ten. The reason for this success is how seamlessly the movie weaves the classic themes of the original fairy tale with an updated story that gives all the characters more developed personalities, resulting in a believable romance and both Belle and Beast ranking among Disney's best characters. The animation is also beautiful, the songs by Menken and Ashman iconic, and its message about change and seeing what lies beneath the surface is timeless.

Sebastien Pocognoli notices major difference in key Scotland man Ben Gannon-Doak

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Sebastien Pocognoli is delighted his prediction that Ben Gannon-Doak will enjoy a lot more action for Bournemouth the longer the season goes on has come true in recent weeks.

There were concerns following the last international window that the winger, who started two of Scotland’s four Nations League matches, would suffer from going back into cold storage on the south coast.

However, Pocognoli expressed confidence that manager Marco Rose would utilise the winger more freely given the club’s European commitments.

Gannon-Doak has since featured seven times for Bournemouth, including four starts. He was even named man-of-the-match in the recent 3-0 Europa League win over AC Milan that shocked so many people across Europe. Many had doubted whether he could contribute sustained performances for Scotland while featuring so rarely for Bournemouth.

But Pocognoli, who is hoping to secure his first Hampden victory on Friday night against North Macedonia, is comforted that his forecast that Gannon-Doak would return to England and “smash it” has proved accurate.

“Ben’s intensity is totally different from when he arrived in the camp in La Manga (in September),” he said at Hampden on Tuesday. “You saw his press against AC Milan or in the other Europa League games, it’s totally different fitness now.

“I am very happy with him and I believe that if we are playing good football it doesn’t matter the shape, he’s in the best position. He’s a young lad, twenty-years-old, so he needs confidence and to feel the love.

“He’s a top player and top players want to improve every day. I like his mentality, it’s exciting to see it but what’s the most exciting is that he’s still to improve on many aspects of the game. If he can improve on being more clinical in front of goal he would probably have scored two goals in the last window.”

Pocognoli has noticed the player is happier taking on responsibility and is displaying more composure. He is thinking more about what to do next when he has the ball rather than absent mindedly throwing it into the box.

“His assist and crossing was more with the idea before ‘if I put a cross in Scott McTominay will make a bicycle kick!’” he said. “But you see a little bit more of him now thinking what the next action is when he’s dribbling. When he’s more efficient, I think he will be a different level. But he’s only 20 so it’s (already) quite exciting.”

Although it was a slightly underwhelming first international window for Pocognoli, the Belgian believes players such as Gannon-Doak reaped the benefits from the four games.

Scotland won once against North Macedonia, lost 3-0 to Switzerland and drew and lost against Slovenia.

“I said to the lads when they arrived in September there were a lot of players with a lack of minutes,” he recalled.

“I said that when European football arrives they’ll probably share it out more. I’m sure the last camp allowed a lot of players to go back to their clubs (with a bit of) bounce.

"If you compare it to the first camp, there are maybe only two or three players with us now who have been playing less minutes but the rest have continued (to play more). I think the camp helped them build the confidence and get some minutes.”

Elisha Goldstein: A Nourishing Breath Meditation

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The most trusted resource for mindfulness and meditation.

The deepest healing occurs when you come to terms with the way things are. This simple awareness of breath practice is an act of self-love and compassion for the moment we find ourselves in.

Mindful breathing often serves as the foundation for meditation practices because your breath is always with you, wherever you are, and it can be used as an anchor to the present moment. In essence, all that’s involved is simply being mindful when you breathe in and out. There is no need to analyze, count, visu­alize, or manipulate the breath in any way. Just breathe normally and naturally and be aware of breathing in and out. There are a few methods you can use to focus on your breath. You can be mindful of your breath in your nose, chest, belly, or even your entire body as it breathes in and out.

With any of these practices, the deepest healing occurs when you come to terms with the way things are.

For dealing with the challenges of stress and anxiety, we sometimes recommend abdominal breath­ing—breathing from the belly, rather than only into the chest—as this can be very calming. However, if another location is preferable, please listen to your own wisdom. Generally speaking, abdominal or belly breathing is the way we all naturally breathe, especially when we’re lying down. To determine if you’re breathing from your abdomen, place your hand on your belly and feel whether it expands as you inhale and contracts as you exhale. If it doesn’t, turn your attention to breathing more deeply and feeling your belly expand and contract with your breath.

An important benefit of abdominal or belly breathing is that it helps moderate irregular breathing patterns, which often arise due to stress or irritation. Anxiety can lead to shallow, rapid, or sporadic breathing and even hyperventilation, and a full-blown panic attack can cause increased shortness of breath, thoughts of losing control, and pains in the chest. By bringing the breath back into the belly, you can help the body return to balance. So when anxiety arises, first acknowledge the feeling, then gently bring attention to the abdomen and practice mindful belly breathing.

Before we begin, we have one final bit of advice: With any of these practices, the deepest healing occurs when you come to terms with the way things are. This might mean simply noticing and acknowledging stress or anxiety rather than falling into old patterns of running away from it. You may discover that by embracing your fear you find your heart.

Do this practice in a relaxing environment without distractions, such as the phone. You can do it either lying down or sitting up, but if you lie down and find yourself falling asleep, try a more upright posture. Bring your full, undivided attention to this practice as you listen or read the following meditation, pausing after each paragraph. You can practice anytime throughout the day, combining this practice with the mindful check-in if you like.

Read and practice the guided meditation script below, pausing after each paragraph. Or listen to the audio practice.

This practice excerpted from A Mindfulness-Based Stress Reduction Workbook, by Bob Stahl and Elisha Goldstein. New Harbinger Publications

Elisha Goldstein, Ph.D., is a clinical psychologist and conducts a private practice in West Los Angeles. He is founder of the Mindful Living Collective—a donation-based leading global network for mindful living and learning, and creator of the Uncover the Power Within coaching program and the free master class on the Uncover the Power Within Blueprint. He is also the author of Uncovering Happiness: Overcoming Depression with Mindfulness and Self-Compassion (Atria Books, 2015), The Now Effect (Atria Books, 2012), Mindfulness Meditations for the Anxious Traveler (Atria Books, 2013), and co-author of A Mindfulness-Based Stress Reduction Workbook (New Harbinger, 2010).

Inside Tommy Lee Jones’ 6,000-Acre Texas Ranch Complete With 2 Polo Fields

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Texas native Tommy Lee Jones moved to New York City to pursue an acting career back in 1969, but he knew he had found real success when he realized he could head back to the Lone Star State.

“There was a time when I became aware that I could live anywhere I want to,” “The Fugitive” star told The New York Times in a profile. “So, in my wisdom, I went home.”

These days, “home” for the 80-year-old actor is a sprawling ranch in San Saba, TX. Jones resides on the 6,000-square-foot property with his wife, Dawn Laurel-Jones, 62, who is from San Antonio.

In addition to a two-story limestone residence that was built as a fort in 1856, the massive plot of land is a full-fledged retreat. There is not one, but two polo fields, and an array of animals from horses to dogs to feral pigs.

The property is a fully operable ranch, and in 2006, Jones told Texas Monthly all about what a typical day is like.

"In the summers, we'll work cattle, and then at about 7 o'clock at night, we'll start playing polo," he said. "We'll play until about 9, and then when we're done, we put the meat on the fire and watch the dark come. It's a good life. Everyone is happy."

Although Jones is famously private and has not shared imagery of the home, he did share a quote that piques interest.

“I didn’t want any fences around the house,” he said while telling the Times of the feral hogs that dig up his yard. “As you can see, the house has an architectural statement to make.”

Inside, the abode is just as fascinating. The publication reported that the living room is furnished with two Frank Lloyd Wright slate-top tables and a Gustav Stickley sofa that is more than a century old.

Antiques and collected art pieces can be found throughout the home, as well as other finds from the property itself, such as flint arrowheads, shed antlers, and small animal skulls.

Jones has ridden horses for most of his life and took an interest in polo in the 1970s. He regularly hosts polo matches at his ranch, which is home to 25 horses.

According to Social Life Magazine, Jones has been expanding his cattle ranch for decades after first acquiring it in the 1980s. He has built onto the property with additional parcels of working ranchland over time.

The couple’s passion for polo has made an impression on more than one of their homes. In 2019, Jones offloaded his Florida equestrian estate for $11.5 million. The 55-acre property, known as “San Saba,” the same name as his hometown, is 72 miles north of Miami in a town called Wellington.

While that residence was considerably smaller than the Texas ranch he lives in today, its polo facilities are remarkable.

Bradley A. Scherer, the listing agent who handled the sale, told Mansion Global that the estate was “one of the only, if not the only, property listed during 2019 that was a true, full-service polo facility with existing polo fields.”

Jones had purchased the Florida estate in 2002 for $2.95 million, then acquired a secondary parcel for $550,000 in 2012. With an additional third parcel, the land was all sold in a single transaction, after it lingered on the market for eight years.

The original asking price was $26.75 million.
