I think China just taught a lesson about AI that US audiences really should learn

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

And I'm not talking about Nvidia-related spycraft.

One of the wildest entertainment stories of the year was Niu Lai, a Chinese animated film that immediately became a laughing stock for its incredibly rudimentary animation and character models.

And even though taste is in the eye of the beholder, I find it very hard to believe anyone would like this at face value. “Why is something that looks worse than many Newgrounds animations from the early ’00s even showing in theaters?” You might ask, but it turns out Niu Lai was a family’s labor of love that lucked into a modest distribution. This crucial piece of information turned the tide and got people to see the film in droves, giving it a $9 million theatrical run on a pretty much non-existent budget.

And while one could argue that a lot of people only started to care about the film because they learned the story about its creation, well, that’s completely fair, because film is a vessel for connection.

Niu Lai‘s story becomes even more interesting when you realize China recently saw the release of Sanxingdui: Future Past, the country’s first fully AI movie release, which bombed hard. Sure, it could perhaps make a comeback, but it can’t count on its development tale to pull the heartstrings of moviegoers because its production, like any other AI production, amounts to that of a bunch of guys using a computer to type the plot of the movie they wanted to rip off.

A studio spent $1.5 million on the first-ever theatrical AI film, which ended up grossing 100.000 dollars. A very different outcome from that of the millions of LinkedIn users who claim to have only spent $100 on a Lord of the Rings ripoff too ludicrous to even imagine.

Westerners with only one photo in their profile and no background to speak of often claim China’s population shows way more openness to the prospects of an AI-led future than the people of the West, but that might, fortunately, be bunk.

Unfortunately, however, the theatrical run of Wildwood shows that the West might’ve become too desensitized even for the most amazing displays of true animation prowess of all time. Yes, despite being arguably the most impressive animated film I’ve ever witnessed, and also being the result of many intense years of loving work, Laika’s epic also failed to even recoup its budget. Honestly, I’d normally advise you against seeing the trailer, but if that’s what’ll earn it a few more ticket sales, then so be it:

On top of being as far from using AI as a movie can get, Wildwood is also the best-rated animated movie in a decade. As far as technique and spectacle go, it’s perfectly reasonable to call this the Avatar or even the Star Wars of stop-motion movies—and a work capable of going toe to toe with any live-action or traditional ( or CGI) animated film, but people just didn’t care, or didn’t know.

Yeah, the marketing team either didn’t have the resources, or didn’t do a great job. Still, the stellar reactions to Wildwood should’ve at least granted it a healthy word-of-mouth-fed growth, but it’s yet to pass the $70 million mark worldwide, a number that stops very short of the movie’s budget—and truth is, the real budget has never even been officially revealed, but that can only mean it’s that big. And, if you’ve seen the movie, you’ll know there’s no way Laika studio pulled this off with the $100 million budget that some people were floating around.

It’s great to see Niu Lai succeed while an AI-made film crashes and burns, but it’d have been even better if that beautiful event had also sent shockwaves that got people to watch more beautifully crafted stuff, because we might never get to see anything like Wildwood again. Even if that means we might also not be seeing many more AI-made movies, either.

Tiago is a freelancer who used to write about video games, cults, and video game cults. He now writes for Destructoid in an attempt to find himself on the winning side when the robot uprising comes.

Christopher Nolan's The Odyssey Officially Sets Home Release Date

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

After a $1.8 billion run at the global box office, Christopher Nolan's The Odyssey is coming home. Universal Pictures Home Entertainment confirmed that Nolan's adaptation of Homer's epic will get a digital and physical release in November, letting viewers relive The Odyssey at home — now with "exclusive content never-before-seen by audiences."

Universal says that The Odyssey's home video release will arrive "months" before the film comes to streaming services (likely NBCUniversal's Peacock), confirming a 2027 debut on streaming.

The Odyssey will officially be released on digital platforms, 4K Ultra HD, Blu-ray, and DVD on Nov. 17.

The home release will include more than 90 minutes of special features, which were "personally overseen by Christopher Nolan," Universal said in a news release. The collection of bonus features includes behind-the-scenes footage, interviews with the cast and crew, and the film's IMAX-exclusive prologue. Some of the film's special features will be exclusive to The Odyssey's physical releases.

* Denotes features only available on physical formats

The Odyssey was written and directed by Christopher Nolan. The movie stars Matt Damon, Tom Holland, Anne Hathaway, Robert Pattinson, Lupita Nyong’o, and Samantha Morton, with Zendaya and Charlize Theron. They are joined by John Leguizamo, Jon Bernthal, Himesh Patel, Bill Irwin, Elliot Page, and Benny Safdie.

Nolan's adaptation of Homer's epic was both a box office success — it's the second-highest-grossing film of 2026, behind Spider-Man: Brand New Day — and a critical darling. In Polygon's review of The Odyssey, entertainment editor Jake Kleinman called it "one of the most epic and beautiful films of all time."

The Odyssey, Kleinman wrote, is Nolan "at the height of his power, using every skill, trick, technique, and ounce of Hollywood cred at his disposal to tell a story that's both awe-inspiring and shockingly unambiguous. With a sprawling cast of stars, jaw-dropping special effects, inexplicable visuals, and a masterful score from Ludwig Göransson, The Odyssey is Nolan's purest picture ever. It's his Lord of the Rings trilogy boiled down to one three-hour saga, a meticulous tribute to a beloved story told with just the right mix of fealty and creative liberty to forge something timely and timeless all at once."

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

"I don't appreciate it," the singer said. "This is a safe space."

One person’s rude gestures in the crowd at sombr‘s recent concert made the musician feel, well, somber.

As captured in clips from the night, the pop star paused his Sunday show in Columbus, Ohio, to express his dismay over a man in attendance who apparently wouldn’t stop giving sombr the middle finger while he was performing. “[He] just keeps flipping me off and flipping me off every night,” the singer/songwriter said.

“Man, I don’t know why you’re coming to my concert if you’re gonna keep flipping me off,” he continued. “It’s all love, but please, please don’t flip me off at my show. It’s this guy with the shades and the leather jacket right here. … It’s really making me sad, man. I don’t appreciate it. This is a safe space. I want everyone here to be kind to each other.”

It’s unclear if sombr and the culprit were able to go back to friends after the exchange, but in general, the hitmaker has had his share of experience dealing with negativity. He previously made headlines when he defended himself against a person who posted a viral TikTok bashing his concert performance and appearance, and in September, sombr passed along the advice Taylor Swift gave him on the subject.

“One of the best pieces of advice the legend has given me … is that if these hate comments go away, maybe you’re not at your peak anymore,” he told Elle. “I think it’s a privilege to be receiving these and rise above it. Ever since she gave me that advice, it’s really changed my perspective and has helped.”

The star is currently on tour in support of his Billboard 200 No. 10 debut album I Barely Know Her, with dates scheduled through summer 2027. The flipper-offer in the crowd in Columbus wasn’t the only unexpected interruption that’s taken place so far on the trek, with sombr taking a pause during his Nov. 3 show in Milwaukee after he chipped his tooth on his water bottle mid-performance.

“Oh, sh–! I chipped my tooth again!” he said at the time. “I’m so happy to inform all of you that ‘Thombr’ has returned.”

Disney+ Has Failed To Reverse Moana's $250 Million Disappointment

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Disney+ has become a place for Disney's biggest movies to become even more popular or finally find a wide audience after initially releasing in theaters. The streaming service has propped up major blockbusters like Avatar: The Way of Water and Zootopia 2, while it gave a box office bomb like Tron: Ares some redemption.

The latter category is where Disney believed 2026's Moana would fall. The live-action remake of the beloved 2016 animated movie came out in theaters this summer, and brought significant box office expectations with it. The animated Moana movies were massive hits on the big screen, with Moana 2 making over $1 billion just two years ago.

The live-action remake could not go nearly as far. The $250 million production bombed with a $323 million worldwide gross. After the film's poor opening weekend, Moana was projected to lose over $100 million for Disney. Rather than immediately declaring the remake a misfire, Disney's new CEO Josh D'Amaro suggested that it brought in substantial revenue elsewhere and believed it would draw significant interest on Disney+.

Moana entered its streaming era on October 21, which is when it officially debuted on Disney's service worldwide. This should have been the start of a successful run for the movie as a global audience enjoyed the film at home, and despite finding some more fans, the reception on Disney+ has failed to reverse the movie's disappointing run.

At first glance, it's easy to think that the live-action remake has been a hit. Moana found streaming success on Disney+ immediately, as it climbed to #1 on the charts. Nearly three weeks later, it still holds this spot globally (via FlixPatrol). However, whereas it was the biggest movie in 71 countries then, it is down to only 14 now, one of them being the United States.

Moana's chart-topping performance is not as good as it seems thanks to its viewership numbers. Disney has not officially announced any details about how the remake has performed in its debut. The silence speaks volumes in a case like this. Disney loves to celebrate its biggest streaming hits and announce when new films do great on the streamer. Clearly, the Disney+ viewership is not worth bringing to light for Disney.

This isn't just an assumption, as Nieslen's data backs this up. The global service tracks viewership across all streamers every week, and from October 19–25 (the week Moana debuted on Disney+), the movie was watched for 579 million minutes. That is equivalent to just over 5 million views based on the film's 115-minute runtime. While that was enough for Moana to be the biggest movie on streaming that week, it was poor for Disney.

As reported by Netflix & Chiffres, the 5 million views in its first 5 days on Disney+ give Moana one of the lowest launches for a live-action Disney theatrical movie on the streamer. It just edged out The Fantastic Four: First Steps and was ahead of Freakier Friday and Tron: Ares, but that's where the list ends.

This makes Moana the worst Disney+ launch for a Disney live-action remake. The Little Mermaid doubled its viewership (10.2 million views). Lilo & Stitch performed well with 7.4 million views. Even Snow White debuted to 5.3 million views. This lack of interest at launch doesn't inspire confidence that Moana's additional time on the streamer will right the ship, putting the franchise in uncharted waters.

The animated sequel has also been a massive success during its time on streaming. Disney announced last year that Moana 2 launched to 27.5 million views in its first 5 days, which was the third-largest debut for a theatrical movie at the time. It went on to become Disney+'s most-watched movie in 2025, earning 80 million views (via What's On Disney Plus).

Both Moana movies charted on Disney+ ahead of the live-action remake's release, adding to their impressive viewership figures. It was anticipated that the 2026 feature would have a similar level of popularity once it came to streaming. Instead, the live-action Moana received less than 20% of the initial views that Moana 2 did less than two years ago.

As a result, Disney+ has not been the saving grace for Moana that everyone associated with it hoped for. It has simply been another opportunity for audiences to largely reject the remake. Now, the 2026 movie will go down as a critical, financial, and streaming disappointment.

The results are surprising for the Moana franchise, but there were questions about its viability as a remake from early on. Many of these were diminished due to the assumption that general audiences would still be eager to see Moana and Maui's adventure in this new format, which proved to not be the case.

The biggest hurdle the live-action remake faced was timing. Disney changed a Moana Disney+ show into Moana 2 to give the studio a major box office hit, even though the live-action remake was already announced and gearing up to release. While the remake was delayed a year to space out the releases a bit more, it's clear audiences weren't ready for this type of movie. Not only did they just enjoy an animated sequel, but the original hit was only a decade old, eliminating the nostalgic pull of remaking something like Lilo & Stitch.

It didn't help that 2026's Moana received largely negative reviews, which primarily revolved around its lack of imagination and being far too similar to the original. Dwayne Johnson's decline as a reliable box office star in recent years was also a knock on the movie, but this would've been a bigger hit for him and Disney if the final product was better and came out at a different point in time.

It's worth noting it came out a week before The Odyssey and three weeks before Spider-Man: Brand New Day. Those became the event films of the summer, and with families showing more favor to Minions & Monsters (which came out just 9 days before) and Toy Story 5 (released one month before), the stacked summer competition proved to be too much for Moana to combat.

Now, the question becomes what comes next for Moana. The IP is too valuable to Disney for them to let it die. Just as Moana was brought back to life in Moana 2's ending, the franchise will live on despite the disappointing outcome this year.

This will come with an extra focus on animation versus exploring more live-action avenues. Dwayne Johnson confirmed Moana 3 is in development ahead of the live-action remake's release. He revealed that Jared Bush and Dana Ledoux Miller are writing the script for Walt Disney Animation Studios, which has yet to formally announce plans or a release date for the third animated film.

What 2026 really means for Moana is that we won't be seeing any more live-action remakes. Disney isn't going to try and redo the animated sequel right now, with Moana 3 the priority. By the time that movie is ready to come out, and the studio even begins contemplating potential next steps in the live-action space, Catherine Laga'aia will have aged out of the teenager role. Something like a live-action Maui prequel could have happened in the interim if the 2026 movie was successful, but that's not getting a greenlight now.

CoreWeave Delivers Another Blowout Quarter. Is It Enough To Save the AI Trade?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

CoreWeave topped estimates, but balance sheet worries linger.

CoreWeave(CRWV -0.51%), the largest of the neoclouds, tumbled over the last month, dragging down the AI sector with it, after a social media post showed that its bond yields had reached as high as 15%. That observation set off another round of concerns that the AI build-out is unsustainable, and the issue has only been magnified since Democrats swept the midterm elections, in part by campaigning on increased regulation for data centers.

Against that backdrop, CoreWeave reported third-quarter results Tuesday afternoon, and the company made it clear that demand for its services, which essentially consist of renting AI compute, continues to skyrocket.

Revenue in the quarter jumped 168% to $3.66 billion, ahead of the analyst consensus at $3.54 billion. CoreWeave's revenue backlog improved from $104 billion at the end of the second quarter to $132.5 billion at the end of the third, another sign of strong demand.

Its generally accepted accounting principles (GAAP) operating margin also improved from 4% to 6% as GAAP operating income jumped from $52 million to $207 million.

However, CoreWeave has taken on tens of billions in debt to fund its rapid data center expansion and chip purchases, which have continued to weigh on the bottom line. Interest expense jumped from $310 million in the quarter a year ago to $846 million, and it finished the quarter with a GAAP net loss of $709 million, or $1.27 per share. That compared with expectations of a loss of $1.21 per share. On an adjusted basis, its net loss expanded from $41 million to $529 million, but improved slightly on a sequential basis.

On a free cash flow basis, CoreWeave lost $7.7 billion in the quarter, and its debt jumped to $47 billion.

Investors mostly shrugged off the earnings report, with the stock gaining modestly in after-hours trading, up 2% before its earnings call at 5 p.m. ET.

The report didn't seem to change many opinions on the stock, and that's understandable. CoreWeave reported another round of soaring revenue growth, but investors expected that, and it didn't show it was turning the corner on its balance sheet risk yet.

However, management said it would not use the unsecured debt market at current interest rates, which offers some encouragement to investors worried about its rising bond yields.

CoreWeave expects interest expense to continue to rise into the fourth quarter, reaching $1.05 billion-$1.15 billion on $4.7 billion-$5 billion in revenue as it raised its full-year revenue forecast to $13 billion-$13.3 billion. The fourth-quarter forecast implies revenue more than tripling.

On the earnings call, CEO Michael Intrator argued that prices for older GPUs had been resilient and were in fact rising, even for Nvidia chips that are three generations old.

That should offer some reassurance to investors, as should the company's plan to collect $9 billion in customer prepayments and deposits in the fourth quarter.

Still, the Fed is expected to raise interest rates at its next meeting in December after a hot Consumer Price Index report. CoreWeave has a current ratio below 0.5, and its interest expense is more than 20% of its revenue.

Its revenue growth is impressive, and its prospects are bright, but investors are understandably wary given current interest rates.

Overall, CoreWeave is making prudent decisions, and operating leverage is improving. With time, the stock should recover its recent losses if it remains on this path.

For the AI sector more broadly, from CoreWeave’s perspective, demand looks set to outstrip supply for years to come, and the company believes it has several levers to pull to fund the build-out. That should reassure any fears about a string of defaults crushing the industry.

Alaska Airlines Launches Invite-Only Global Concierge Status To Rival US Big Three

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Alaska Airlines has introduced Atmos Global Concierge, an invitation-only elite status program that recognizes its most valuable customers with personalized travel assistance and exclusive benefits. The new offering sits above the existing Atmos Rewards Titanium tier and brings the Seattle-based carrier closer to American Airlines, Delta Air Lines, and United Airlines, all of which operate their own invitation-only loyalty programs. Invitations began reaching selected Atmos Rewards members on November 9, 2026, with membership currently extending through December 31, 2027. The initiative comes as Alaska continues to expand its international network and strengthen its position in the premium travel market.

The new status is a curious case because Alaska has not published qualification requirements or established a publicly accessible application process. Instead, the airline appears to be selecting travelers based on their overall value to its business, with early reports suggesting that expensive international business class bookings could play an important role. Recipients are also being offered complimentary Titanium status, alongside a collection of services intended to make long-haul journeys more convenient. Although the program remains in its early stages, it represents another important development in Alaska's evolving loyalty strategy.

Atmos Global Concierge provides invited members with access to a dedicated support team responsible for assisting them throughout their journeys. According to information shared by recipients on Reddit, the benefits include proactive flight monitoring, assistance during travel disruptions, and a next-flight guarantee intended to help passengers reach their destinations when their original itineraries are disrupted. Members can also receive arrival assistance at selected long-haul destinations across Europe, Asia, and Oceania. Another benefit is car transfers at London Heathrow Airport (LHR) for passengers with tight connections.

The reported package also includes upgrade priority above regular Titanium members, particularly on international flights, alongside complimentary CLEAR+ membership and Avis Chairman's Club status. These benefits combine airport assistance, ground transportation, and additional travel privileges rather than focusing exclusively on earning more points or accessing airport lounges. According to One Mile at a Time, recipients can retain their new status through December 31, 2027. Alaska has also confirmed the broader purpose of the initiative, stating the following to Simple Flying:

“Atmos™ Global Concierge is a new, invitation-only program that, in addition to all the award-winning benefits of Atmos Rewards membership, provides personalized travel support when you need it the most. Members are supported by a dedicated Concierge team throughout their journey, including proactive trip monitoring and travel assistance, along with exclusive benefits and curated experiences. Through this beta program, we’re creating new ways to recognize some of our most loyal members as our global network grows.”

The introduction of Global Concierge places Alaska alongside the three largest US network carriers in offering exclusive recognition to selected customers. American Airlines operates ConciergeKey, Delta Air Lines has Delta 360, while United Airlines offers Global Services. These programs are generally reserved for travelers considered especially valuable to their respective airlines, with spending patterns, premium cabin bookings, and corporate travel relationships often believed to influence selection. Qualification criteria are typically confidential, making invitations hard to predict even for frequent flyers.

Early reactions from Atmos Rewards members suggest that Alaska may be prioritizing the value of individual bookings rather than simply rewarding the highest number of status points. Reports shared on Reddit indicate that some recipients regularly purchase international business class tickets, while other members with substantial status-point balances have not received invitations. This suggests the airline may be trying to attract customers who might otherwise spend heavily with competing international carriers.

The distinction between Global Concierge and conventional elite status is that it appears to emphasize personalized assistance rather than simply offering additional mileage bonuses or airport privileges. For passengers traveling internationally, having an airline team monitor their itineraries and arrange alternatives during disruptions could be very valuable. A missed connection at a major European hub, for example, can create significant complications when passengers have connecting flights operated by different carriers. Alaska's reported Heathrow transfer benefit also indicates that the airline is looking beyond its own aircraft operations to improve the overall travel experience.

The initiative also reflects a broader effort to retain premium customers as Alaska expands its international operations. Following its acquisition of Hawaiian Airlines, the company has gained access to a substantially broader network and greater opportunities to compete for long-haul passengers. Recognizing travelers who regularly purchase expensive international tickets could help strengthen loyalty as Alaska develops additional services and competes with established global airlines.

The new program brings a lot to the table.

Alaska has described Global Concierge as a beta program designed to develop new ways to recognize its most loyal customers as its global network grows. In a comment explaining the initiative, the airline said the new offering supplements existing Atmos Rewards benefits with personalized support, proactive travel monitoring, exclusive privileges, and curated experiences. The company also highlighted the importance of having a dedicated team available throughout each member's journey, particularly when unexpected travel complications arise. This suggests that Alaska sees the program as more than another loyalty designation, positioning it instead as a premium service offering that could evolve as the airline gathers feedback.

However, several questions about the program remain unanswered. Alaska has not disclosed how customers are selected, whether it will issue invitations regularly, or what spending levels might make someone eligible. The precise conditions surrounding the reported next-flight guarantee and Heathrow transfers also remain unclear. Furthermore, the benefits currently reported by members do not indicate whether Global Concierge will add lounge access, separate boarding privileges, or improved award availability.

The move nevertheless arrives at an important time for Alaska Airlines. Its expanding international ambitions require the carrier to compete not only through fares and destinations but also through the quality of its premium passenger experience. Atmos Global Concierge gives Alaska another tool to attract high-value travelers who might otherwise favor the US Big Three when booking expensive international journeys. Whether the beta program eventually becomes a permanent fixture of Atmos Rewards remains to be seen, but its introduction signals that Alaska intends to compete more directly for the most lucrative segment of the US airline loyalty market.

Ted Sarandos Faces Unrest From Past & Present Netflix Execs Over Streamer’s Direction – The Dish

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Executive Editor, Legal, Labor & Politics

Bela Bajaria and 1,000 Netflix staffers have been let go, and Peter Rice has parachuted in as the streamer’s content chief. But, the real change at the streamer may still be to come, as Deadline has learned. As a coterie of mostly former and some current Netflix executives have banded together in opposition to co-CEO Ted Sarandos‘ strategy for the company..

Meeting on WhatsApp chains, Griffith Park hikes, and the Godfrey Hotel Hollywood, the disenchanted Netflix senior staffers past and present essentially break down into two camps. One group is terrified by the double digit declining stock, both for the company and their own bank accounts. The other is troubled by what they see as a fundamental move away from the original form of Netflix’s notorious kill-or-be-killed culture. Related Stories Books Everything We Know About Netflix's 'Better Than The Movies' Feature Adaptation So Far Acquisitions Netflix Developing Wedding-Themed Romantic Dramedy Series From 'Hart Of Dixie's Leila Gerstein

Along with the group’s lack of a unifying high-profile leader, that split focus is the heart of the inertia that’s so far stifling the dissenters in the weeks since the latest purge. But the culture conflict appears to be a stronger sore point than the stock slide among the ex-VP crowd, sources say. Watch on Deadline

With repeated assertions that “Ted has gone Hollywood,” the former and current executives say they believe Netflix has “lost its mojo” in the past few years. Specifically, they assert the streamer has stumbled in moving from being a harsh workplace based on performance to “just another studio” where politics are the “key” factor for success. Those politics primarily are said to play out via proximity to Sarandos, and being on board with his love of prestige programming, big names, and Old Hollywood.

Still, the stock slide is no small concern. Netflix shares are down more than 45% compared with a year ago. At the close of the market October 30, Netflix’s shares were at $62.10, their lowest level since August 2024. They ended Tuesday’s trading at $63.26.

“The stock slipping below $60, plus a weak awards season could be fatal,” an ex-Netflix VP told Deadline of a scenario that may see Sarandos’ leadership openly questioned. “Ted’s not the Pope, the job’s not for life.”

Still, as veteran media analyst Rich Greenfield of Lightshed Partners noted in an October 19 blog post: “Given Ted’s longstanding role in shaping Netflix’s content strategy/philosophy, why should investors believe the problem was Bela and Dan’s execution rather than the broader strategy set by Ted?”

A well-placed individual told Deadline that the leadership and structural changes of last month were never intended to move the stock upward, but instead to steady the business.

While the dissenters have yet to make a direct move against Sarandos, they are circling in hopes that events — or “an intervention,” as one insider termed it — will force Sarandos to “shift his priorities significantly.” The onetime Cassettes West store manager has been at Netflix for 26 years, six of them as co-CEO with company co-founder Reed Hastings, and now co-CEO with Greg Peters.

For now, most of the dissent and backlash is directed at Sarandos, rather than Peters, who is viewed very much as the tech guy to Sarando’s content guy. Still, while Sarandos is the first among equals between the duo, Peters, who was not entirely in sync with Sarandos on the WBD acquisition bid, has had some less than stellar results of his own out of Netflix’s investment in video games and the recent tech fail with advertising.

To that, the discussion (early discussion, it should be stated) among numerous dissenters would see a “heavy hitter” go to company co-founder Hastings and persuade him that Netflix needs a blood transfusion at the top. The Hail Mary hope is that Hastings, who stepped away from the streamer as executive chairman officially in June, would then go to the Jay Hoag-led board and recommend a leadership change.

That’s a lot of moving parts to a plan that is still very much in a preliminary stage.

While the white collar grassroots angst is considerable, but it hasn’t yet become a full-scale corporate governance issue. In most situations when a CEO faces scrutiny, there are two scenarios: an activist investor buying up shares and waging a proxy war, or the board of directors hitting the “eject” button and brings in a new leader.

In terms of a messenger for the would-be heretics, various big shot old skool Netflix names have been being floated. In those discussions, the three names that keep coming up are now ex-Paramount+ head Cindy Holland, former Head of Project Todd Yellin, and ex-documentary and indie flicks boss Lisa Nishimura.

“It has to be someone Reed respects, someone he will hear out,” a former Netflix executive tells Deadline.

It’s no surprise the trio of Netflix OGs received outreach from old colleagues when the streamer axed Bajaria, film boss Dan Lin and 6% of its workforce last month. Yet, there is no indication so far that Holland (who left being Netflix’s Original Content VP in 2020), Yellin (who segued from the C-Suite in 2022 to being a producer on the likes of the upcoming 52nd State) and Nishimura (who exited the streamer after 16 years in 2023 and is an independent docu producer now) offered anything more than words of comfort and condolence.

Though some of the three are said to be receptive to the opposition to the current Netflix regime, none of them are formally part of the dissenters, I’m also told.

Reading the perceived smoke signals rising from that podcast appearance, a number of ex-Netflix execs that Deadline spoke since indicated if there was anyone who could get Hastings’ direct attention on the concerns they have, it would be Todd Yellin. Having worked together at the streamer starting in 2006, Hastings and Yellin are still close personally and professionally In fact, the ex-co-CEO and board chair became a producer on the Yellin co-written and directed thriller 52nd State in July 2025, his first foray into such a credit.

The desire for Hastings’ direct involvement increased even more among the dissenters after the defensive Sarandos and Peter earnings call of October 20, that saw the stock dip in after-hours trading. Interestingly, soon after that, Hastings showed up on Kara Swisher’s podcast. In the October 25 episode, Hastings told the former CNN regular that while he’d “like to be in the fray helping,” he felt “competent people” were running Netflix and “they’ll figure it out.”

Hastings did not respond to a request for comment from Deadline on concerns about Netflix’s direction. Contacted by Deadline, Netflix had no comment on the concerns about the state of the streamer.

A source close to the company says Sarandos, Peters and Hastings maintain a strong relationship. The same source also insisted there are no plans for Hastings to return to Netflix.

Steven Spielberg’s 10-Part Sci-Fi Thriller Is Perfect for ‘Person of Interest’ Fans

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

15 years ago, CBS delivered one of its best procedurals with Person of Interest. The series, which focuses on a mysterious "Machine" and a former Special Forces operative tasked by the Machine's creator to protect the people it identifies, perfectly blends a high concept sci-fi pitch with the standard procedural formula. Like Lost before it, Person of Interest saw a wake of similar shows premiering after its debut, most notably Almost Human. One series boasts the pedigree of being a direct sequel to one of the best sci-fi movies of the 21st century.

That series is Minority Report, which picks up in the aftermath of Steven Spielberg's sci-fi thriller. The Minority Report series follows Dash (Stark Sands), a former precognitive whose visions were used by the "Precrime" police department to stop crimes before they happen. Haunted by visions of murders yet to happen, Dash contacts Detective Lara Vega (Meagan Good) to help stop those crimes before they happen. Though it only lasted a single season, Minority Report had similar vibes to Person of Interest, including deep questions about the nature of justice.

Part of the draw of Minority Report is the dynamic between Dash and Vega, especially as she gets used to the fact that he can see the future and transitions into a normal life while still using his abilities to help others. The episode "The Present" is perhaps the strongest example of this dynamic at work, as Dash gets a flash of Vega's death and works to solve it while she deals with the unresolved matter of her father's death. Another element that makes Minority Report stand out is its approach to the future, especially since the series takes place 11 years after the movie. Spielberg was ahead of the curve by asking scientists what technology would look like in the 2050's, and showrunner Max Borenstein said he took a similar approach when envisioning his take on Minority Report.

"One of the refreshing things about the film is that it didn't posit a single catastrophic event that led to the future. It said, "50 years from now, what is the world going to look like?" And the answer was that the world is going to be good and bad in equal measure to today. We have horrible thing that happen in our world, so I don't think we need to conjure up an apocalypse on our show to have real stakes."

Minority Report would also continue to incorporate elements from the movie, including Dash's siblings Arthur (Nick Zano) and Agatha (Laura Reagan). Both of them have wildly different visions; while Arthur gains knowledge of people who are about to be murdered, Agatha ends up psychically stepping in their shoes, which is just as horrifying as it sounds. The duo ends up helping Dash and Vega, but they have to be careful, as mysterious forces are hunting all three precogs to use their abilities. If Person of Interest is a story about the dangers of unchecked technology, then Minority Report asks what the law will do to keep people safe.

Despite being based on one of Steven Spielberg's most iconic movies, and even boasting Spielberg as an executive producer, Minority Report only lasted a single season. The deck was stacked against it from the start, as Fox cut the first season's episode order from thirteen episodes to ten and had it airing in the same time slot as NBC's The Voice, which was at the height of its popularity. Bad reviews didn't help, either, as many critics felt that Minority Report lacked the spark of the movie that inspired it.

The cast and crew of Minority Report has managed to find success with other sci-fi projects. Max Borenstein helped kick off the MonsterVerse movies, Nick Zano would become an integral part of Legends of Tomorrow, and Li Jun Li would go on to headline buzzy projects like Sinners and Spider-Noir. Still, for a series that was about predicting the future, Minority Report never saw its own end coming.

Bitcoin Miner HIVE Eyes $45 Million in Annual Revenue From AI Deal

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin Miner HIVE Eyes $45 Million in Annual Revenue From AI Deal

Bitcoin miner HIVE Digital Technologies is eyeing $45 million in annual revenue from an AI data center in Sweden, but it still hasn't secured a final lease.

In its latest earnings update, HIVE reiterated that its 32-megawatt Big Boden facility could generate the sum through a long-term colocation arrangement. That revenue depends on a definitive agreement, financing, facility upgrades, and the project entering service.

The company first disclosed the proposal in June. It involves an unnamed, investment-grade Swedish technology company and contemplates a lease lasting up to 10 years. During Tuesday's earnings call, CEO Aydin Kilic said the parties were negotiating deposits, termination rights, and other terms that could affect financing costs. A lender's consultant had visited the site the previous week, he said.

“We do not want to rush into a deal just for the sake of reporting new top-line revenue,” Kilic said, according to the earnings call transcript.

HIVE expects to seek financing for the Boden project after securing a signed lease, potentially through corporate bonds. The company has not announced a completion date for negotiations.

The existing data center would also need a substantial makeover. HIVE plans to retrofit it for up to 10,000 Nvidia GB300 GPUs, using liquid cooling to handle high-density computing racks.

Although the facility has a 32-megawatt utility connection, the proposed customer would receive approximately 25 megawatts of usable computing capacity, with the remaining power supporting data center operations.

“This site has evolved from a GPU compute facility supporting Ethereum,” Kilic said when announcing the letter of intent, describing its planned conversion into a more powerful AI installation.

The expansion follows HIVE's broader effort to move beyond Bitcoin mining, a shift that has already produced several large GPU-cloud contracts.

HIVE signed a major three-year AI computing agreement with Bell Canada and Cohere in June, covering 2,304 Nvidia GPUs as it expanded beyond crypto mining.

HIVE now reports $185 million in contracted GPU-cloud annualized run-rate revenue, although only $40 million is live. The remainder represents contracted capacity that has not yet entered service.

The company is targeting approximately $700 million in total high-performance computing annualized revenue by the end of 2028, though that figure includes projects requiring additional financing, construction, and customer commitments.

For Boden, HIVE's next milestone is securing a definitive lease before proceeding with project financing.

Mercor raises $750M at $20B valuation to take AI agent evaluation to enterprises

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Artificial intelligence training data company Mercor.io Corp. today revealed that it has raised $750 million in new funding at a $20 billion valuation.

Mercor provides the human expertise used to train and evaluate AI models, drawing on a network of more than 5 million domain experts. Most of that work so far has been for frontier AI labs. The new funding is aimed at taking it to enterprises in every industry, which Mercor argues need their own way to measure whether an AI agent can be trusted with a job.

Co-Chief Executive Brendan Foody said “every job in the economy” will need its own AI agents and that companies will need a way to train and evaluate them on their own work. Mercor spent the past two years building that infrastructure with the frontier labs. The lab business has grown fast. Foody said in a post on X in July that annualized revenue had passed $2 billion in June.

Adarsh Hiremath, Mercor’s other co-chief executive, said a model can score well on a public benchmark and still fail on “the systems, policies and edge cases that define how a specific business runs.” For each enterprise customer, Mercor builds a custom benchmark that measures models against that company’s own standards. Post-training starts from there.

Part of that post-training runs on software from Deeptune Inc. Mercor bought the New York startup in July, and its team had spent the two years before that recreating hundreds of enterprise applications for AI agents to practice on. The company said the new money will pay for research and evaluation infrastructure, including more of those environments.

Mercor did not name a lead investor for the Series D round, which drew corporate money from Nvidia Corp., Salesforce Inc. and Zoom Communications Inc. Returning investors Benchmark Capital Management Co., Felicis Ventures Management Co. and General Catalyst Group Management also participated. Other investors in the round were Fundomo, G Squared Equity Management LP, Glade Brook Capital Partners, Sapphire Ventures, StepStone Group Inc., XTX Ventures and funds managed by affiliates of Apollo Global Management Inc.

The last round came in October 2025, when Felicis led a $350 million Series C round that valued Mercor at $10 billion. Foody’s blog post on the new round says it included a tender offer for employee shares, and the company has not said how the $750 million splits between that and fresh capital.

Frontier labs have been the main buyers of expert-driven training data so far, according to Jai Das, president, partner and co-founder of Sapphire Ventures. He expects that to change as open models get good enough for enterprises to build on, saying Mercor has built “something hard to replicate” from several years of work with the labs.

Scott McTominay: I didn't know what people were saying about me and the World Cup

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Picture the scene. Scotland fans are drinking Boston’s bars dry. Meanwhile, unbeknown to them, their star player, their main hope, was getting a police escort to a hospital in the city to have his heartbeat jolted back into a normal pattern. Scotland tend to make a drama out of World Cups, but this is something else.

It is almost a year to the day since Scott McTominay wowed the world with an overhead kick to set Scotland on the way to qualifying for the tournament for the first time in 28 years against Denmark. This might have been expected to be a main topic of conversation as the player sat down for an all-too rare interview at Hampden on Tuesday.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

In the end, however, this scintillating piece of skill, immortalised on a tenement gable end near Hampden, proved barely an afterthought. McTominay consents to interviews only very infrequently, certainly in Scotland. When he does do them, they seem like events.

This is especially the case when he is recalling being blue lighted through the streets of Boston a couple of evenings before Scotland were due to make their long-awaited return to the World Cup against Haiti.

It is scarcely believable that having had his heart effectively reset, he was lining up against the Caribbean nation in a pulsating World Cup encounter a mere 36 hours later. He very nearly even scored.

The Napoli midfielder watched a shot crash off the post in the first half shortly before John McGinn scored with a deflected effort that proved the winner. Although he was so close to getting on the scoresheet, the general perception was that McTominay struggled to shake off the effects of the “stomach bug” that had been communicated as the reason for his absence from training two days beforehand.

The midfielder was considered never to have reached the heights expected of him during Scotland’s tournament, which lasted another two games. It must have been excruciatingly irritating for him to read such verdicts and be unable to respond.

He was even rated in some quarters as among the tournament’s biggest flops. The Scotland staff couldn’t say anything, nor could he. He claims he was oblivious to the criticism.

“I didn’t know that’s what people were saying,” he said. “Although I probably had an idea. I’ve been criticised a million times before in my career before but I don’t let it get under my skin or worry too much about it.

“You know yourself as a professional footballer if you’re not quite where you should be. For me, I had half an idea but you just try to block it out because if you think about negative things you might speak it into existence.”

There might be a lot of people eating humble pie having been informed of the true nature of McTominay’s health problem. It’s no one’s fault. You can’t refer to such a serious mitigating factor if it’s being – understandably – kept hidden. McTominay explained to rapt reporters the details behind his condition, which is known as atrial fibrillation. In other words, an unsteady heartbeat. He even provided some sound effects. Who says McTominay is an unwilling interviewee?

“Atrial fibrillation is a fairly normal routine thing for people in general,” he said. “They might not know that they have it. But when you're an elite athlete in general across the board, across football, across sports in general, you know if you've got something that's different.

“Whenever you're running and you check your pulse and it's got a slight irregular beat there, it feels like your heart's coming out of your chest.

“Not obviously as bad as that but that's the sensation that you have. It's pumping, but not a regular heartbeat like bum, bum, bum, bum. Mine's going bum, bu-bu-bum, bum, bu-bu-bum. So you know the difference.”

You also know that it’s not something you would wish to develop on the eve of the World Cup that is being played in intense temperatures.

“The heat was tough, that was probably the hardest part,” he reflected. “But I’ve worked for 20 years as a young footballer and as a professional to get to that point, to try and play well at a World Cup.” Nothing, he stressed, was going to stop him, not even lying on a hospital bed under anaesthetic two evenings before the first game while an electrode is placed on his chest.

He has since been back in hospital for a more involved procedure. Napoli released a statement in September to say the minimally invasive operation known as cardiac ablation surgery had been “a complete success” although McTominay missed several matches, including the first four Nations League fixtures under new manager Sebastien Pocognoli.

“I went into that surgery with no doubt in my mind that I’d be back,” he said. “I lost a little bit of muscle when I came out, three or four kilos which wasn’t ideal. I had to put that back on pretty sharpish.

“The first picture of me when I came back, I looked quite gaunt. So I had to get that muscle back so I could get back my powerful running and what I normally do on a football pitch.

“It was a good reset period for me,” he added. “No matter how long it took - even if it took until I was 40! - I was coming back to try and do my best. I’ve had a good time to reflect on the World Cup and think about things leading up to it.

“When I had the operation I had three weeks at home. I didn’t watch as much football as I normally do. I (normally) watch every game of football, every night. It’s relentless with me.

“Games in the Champions League, Europa League, Scottish football, the Premier League, Serie A - I watch a lot now compared to when I was younger.

“So I had three weeks of resting my mind more than anything to try and come back in a better state.”

It’s good news for the Tartan Army, who are desperate for McTominay, who turns 30 next month, to rescue Scotland from an unsure start to the Nations League under Pocognoli. The Belgian has stressed he wants to find a way of taking the burden off those such as McTominay. But the player himself doesn’t mind the extra responsibility. Indeed, he relishes it.

“He's mentioned that to me before about maybe taking a little bit (of the burden) and putting it across the whole board, but I don’t see football like that,” he said.

“I don’t go into a game thinking that the people watching really need me to do something here so I try to do different things, you just play the game, don’t you?

“Express yourself and play how you would normally play, and the rest will take care of itself, you don’t need to overthink about pressure.

“I’ve played at Man United, I’ve played at Napoli, I know what pressure is. I’ve played for Scotland in the World Cup, I know what pressure is. It’s not the worst thing in the world that can happen to you. It’s a privilege to have things expected of you.”

Pocognoli told reporters last week that he was looking forward to making a connection with someone generally identified as Scotland’s star player. It seems he is already well on the way to achieving this despite the pair only having met in the flesh for the first time earlier this week.

It was Pocognoli's idea for McTominay to sit down with the media at Hampden on Tuesday, an instruction he obeyed.

“I like his ideas a lot,” said McTominay “Whenever I first had a meeting with him, I liked the way that he wants to play the game.”

At the end of the near 15-minute conversation with McTominay, an annual at best occurrence, there was just about enough time to reference the approaching one-year anniversary of That goal. And mention That 36ft tall mural, which was unveiled earlier this year.

“Unreal,” he said. “I don’t know how I feel. I obviously like it, it’s incredible, but if you were asking me, you don’t want my big ugly face up there!”

David Walliams Denies Sexual Misconduct Allegations

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

British comedian David Walliams responded to allegations of sexual misconduct published by The Guardian and ITV, denying claims against him: "I am not a sexual predator. I have not broken the law."

After The Guardian and ITV published a wide-ranging exposé accusing the former Britain's Got Talent judge of sexual misconduct, including sexual harassment, grooming and abusing his power to sexually prey on women and teenagers as young as 15, he broke his silence to vehemently deny the allegations.

"Looking back, I realize that at times I behaved thoughtlessly towards other people I had casual sexual relationships with," Walliams, 55, said in a Nov. 10 statement to the outlets, which joined forces for a three-year joint investigation into the accusations made against him. "I realize I did not always take people's feelings properly into account. That is something I regret."

"Whilst a power imbalance is inevitable when one party is famous, abusing that power is something else entirely and it is something that I never did," the comedian continued. "I am not a sexual predator. I have not broken the law, but I am devastated that some of the women I met 10 to 20 years ago have now expressed hurt or anger towards me." (The age of consent in the U.K. is 16.)

The article was published earlier on Nov. 10, days after a judge rejected Walliams's injunction to prevent its publication. (Walliams, whose attorney said in court he had attempted suicide twice in the months leading up to the publication, per The Guardian, was seeking psychiatric treatment.) Multiple women—who were identified with pseudonyms after agreeing to speak with the publications under the condition of anonymity—accused Walliams of being "predatory" and "pushy" during the alleged interactions beginning in 2003.

Two of his accusers alleged that they met the children's book author when they were minors, including a woman identified as Alex, who said she was 15 when she met him at an autograph signing in 2004 where she gave him her cover letter in hopes of exploring a comedy writing career, according to The Guardian.

Alex alleged that she and Walliams exchanged multiple emails—which were included in the publication—and occasionally spoke on the phone for two years. She said that he suggested that he could help her succeed in the comedy world.

By 17, she said that they were having sex. "I think I was groomed, 100 percent," she told The Guardian. "We met when I was 15 and he was promising to help me and was a figure of authority and someone that I looked to for guidance and help."

Another woman, referred to as Jade, also alleged she was 15 when she had met Walliams. She said that after the then-36-year-old reached out to her on MySpace in 2007—which Jade said clearly showed her date of birth—he invited her to his home, where he kissed her.

Other accounts—ITV and The Guardian cited speaking with 30 sources with first-hand knowledge of his behavior—were from women between 18 and early 20s who alleged Walliams tried to use his fame and wealth to coerce them into sex.

One such accuser, Sarah, 18, alleged that Walliams took an interest in her after meeting him during filming for Britain's Got Talent, according to The Guardian. He eventually invited the starstruck teen to his London home, with her alleging that he attempted to have sex with her while she was drunk and unable to walk properly, but her friend intervened. She said she and her friend ultimately slept in a spare bedroom.

Walliams' attorney emphasized that his client denied any wrongdoing with all of the women, also refuting Sarah's allegation that he got her "drunk to take advantage of her."

E! News has reached out to Walliams' reps and HarperCollins UK for comment and has not yet heard back.

Unspecified allegations were previously made against Walliams—who shares a son with ex Lara Stone—by at least five female employees at his publisher HarperCollins in December 2025, per The Guardian.

The company ultimately dropped him, telling The Telegraph at the time, "After careful consideration, and under the leadership of its new CEO, HarperCollins UK has decided not to publish any new titles by David Walliams."

"The author is aware of this decision," the statement continued. "HarperCollins takes employee wellbeing extremely seriously and has processes in place for reporting and investigating concerns. To respect the privacy of individuals, we do not comment on internal matters."

Walliams's agent told the outlet in a statement that his client "strongly denies that he has behaved inappropriately."

7 Romance Shows That Are 10/10 Masterpieces

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Feel free to connect with him or check out his work. He's everywhere — Upwork, YouTube, Spotify, SoundCloud, Collider, LinkedIn, Instagram.

Romance gets a bad rep for being the guilty pleasure of TV. It's considered as the genre people binge in secret and never put on their "best shows" lists. That reputation is a little unfair, though. A truly great love story is one of the hardest things to pull off on screen. It takes chemistry that can't be faked, and a slow build that makes the audience desperate for two people to finally get it right. When a romance gets all of that right, it becomes something viewers return to again and again, long after they know exactly how it ends.

The shows on this list are the ones that understand romance is really about vulnerability rather than the boring, one-dimensional stereotypes people usually associate with the genre. They give viewers messy, complicated people who have to get out of their own way before they can fall in love. This is a list of romance shows that are 10/10 masterpieces because they do justice to the very act of falling in love.

One Day is a right person, wrong time story that is both heartwarming and unbearable to watch, and that is the mark of a true romance. The series, based on David Nicholls' bestselling novel, begins on July 15, 1988, when Emma Morley (Ambika Mod) and Dexter Mayhew (Leo Woodall) spend one night together after graduating from university in Edinburgh. Every episode then drops in on them on that same date, year after year. The two characters couldn't be any different. Emma is a sharp, working-class aspiring writer who never quite believes she’s good enough, while Dexter is a golden boy who survives on pure charm until his life starts to unravel.

The genius of the show is in its format. Since the audience only sees one day a year, entire relationships and careers happen off-screen, and every episode feels like catching up with an old friend who has changed as life has gone on. Both characters experience genuine growth over the years, and it's impossible not to root for their connection. Just be warned that the final episodes take a turn that hits like a gut punch, and the show ends with a twist that is guaranteed to stay with viewers for days. One Day is the perfect love story about missed chances, and is guaranteed to wreck anyone who makes it to the end.

Nobody expected a Canadian hockey romance to take over the internet, but Heated Rivalry did exactly that within a few weeks. The series, based on Rachel Reid's Game Changers novels and created by Letterkenny's Jacob Tierney, follows two of professional hockey’s biggest young stars. Shane Hollander (Hudson Williams) is a disciplined, focused Canadian, while Ilya Rozanov (Connor Storrie) is a cocky Russian who loves getting under his skin. The two have been rivals since their rookie days in 2008, and on the ice, they can't stand each other. Off the ice, though, they get secretly caught up in an affair that could end both of their careers if anyone found out.

Beneath all the steam, Heated Rivalry is really about two men who have been taught their entire lives that wanting each other could cost them everything. The six-episode season stretches across a decade, so the audience gets to watch a secret fling slowly turn into something neither of them is ready to name. The two are a perfect mismatch, and every stolen moment they get feels charged because things can fall apart at any time. On top of that, the show delivers a second great romance through hockey captain Scott Hunter (François Arnaud) and his boyfriend, Kip (Robbie G.K.), whose story nearly steals the spotlight. Heated Rivalry turned a niche book series into a global obsession, and it has more than earned its place among the great modern love stories.

There are slow burns, and then there's a love story that literally spans two centuries. Outlander, based on Diana Gabaldon's novels, follows World War II nurse Claire Randall (Caitríona Balfe), on her second honeymoon in Scotland who is suddenly thrown back to 1743 after touching an ancient standing stone. Suddenly, Claire finds herself in the middle of a brewing Jacobite rebellion. To protect herself, she is forced to marry Jamie Fraser (Sam Heughan), a young Highland warrior, even though she already has a husband waiting for her in the future.

Unlike most romances, Outlander gets its central couple married early and then asks what happens after they say "I do." Claire and Jamie face wars and impossible choices together, and their love grows deeper with every hardship instead of fading. It's hard not to melt every time Jamie calls her "Sassenach," the nickname that started as a jab and turned into a declaration of love. Claire is also a refreshingly modern heroine, a woman who refuses to stay quiet in a century that expects her to. After eight seasons, Outlander remains the gold standard for epic, sweeping romance on TV.

Bridgerton is Shonda Rhimes' version of Regency, and provides a refreshing take on London high society's ruthless marriage market. The series, based on Julia Quinn's novel series, follows the Bridgerton siblings and their individual love stories. Bridgerton Season 1 centers on Daphne Bridgerton (Phoebe Dynevor), who strikes a deal with the brooding Duke of Hastings (Regé-Jean Page) to fake a courtship, only for real feelings to get in the way. The following seasons follow a new Bridgerton sibling and cover a new romance trope with every season, from enemies-to-lovers to friends-to-lovers, and even a Cinderella-style love story. Of course, overseeing all this drama is the anonymous gossip columnist Lady Whistledown, voiced by Julie Andrews, whose scandal sheet keeps the entire ton on edge.

Bridgerton stands out from other period dramas because of how unapologetically modern it feels. The show reimagines Regency London with a diverse cast and swaps classical music for string-quartet covers of songs by artists including Ariana Grande and Taylor Swift to help the audience relate to the era. Not to mention the series tells its love stories from the women's point of view, which makes the fight for independence just as important as the search for a husband. Bridgerton took the stuffy period romance formula and turned it into a feel-good show that has made period romance cool for a whole new generation.

With The Life of a Showgirl getting an encore edition this week, the era argument is live all over again. Here is yours, what it says about you, and the case for it being the best one she has made.

You are the reinvention. 1989 is the record where she walked away from the thing that made her famous and rebuilt everything from scratch, and it worked so completely that people forgot there had ever been a risk.

You are the one who stopped explaining. Reputation arrived after a year of being written off, with almost no interviews and no attempt to be liked, and it turned out that saying nothing was the loudest available option.

You are the quiet one who was paying attention the whole time. Folklore appeared with less than a day of warning, made entirely indoors, and swapped autobiography for invented characters who somehow felt more personal than the real ones.

You feel everything at maximum volume and you have never apologised for it. Red is the messiest album she has made and the one most people love best, because it is the sound of somebody refusing to be reasonable about how much something hurt.

Heartstopper is proof that a love story doesn't need heartbreak or emotional trauma to make viewers cry. The series, based on Alice Oseman's graphic novels, follows Charlie Spring (Joe Locke), a shy, openly gay student at an all-boys British school. When he's seated next to rugby team star Nick Nelson (Kit Connor), Charlie develops a massive crush, which he knows will never go anywhere. As the two become close friends, though, Nick begins to realize that his feelings for Charlie run much deeper than he expected.

Heartstopper is a show about the small moments. Every awkward "hi" between Charlie and Nick perfectly captures the feeling of a young love and the hand-drawn visual elements in the show make the audience feel like Oseman's novels have come to life. The show is refreshingly kind to its characters, but doesn't shy away from harder topics like bullying and mental health. One of its most moving scenes comes when Nick comes out to his mother, played by Olivia Colman, and it's impossible to get through that without tearing up. Heartstopper is the comforting, joyful queer love story so many viewers wish they had growing up, and it deserves all the appreciation it gets for that.

A South Korean heiress falling for a North Korean soldier sounds like the setup for a joke, but Crash Landing on You makes it work without feeling gimmicky. Yoon Se-ri (Son Ye-jin) is a wealthy, fiercely independent businesswoman who gets swept off course during a paragliding accident and crash-lands on the wrong side of the border. Stern but honorable army officer, Captain Ri Jeong-hyeok (Hyun Bin), finds Se-ri and decides to hide her in his village until she figures out a way home. From there, the story works both as a sweeping romance and a tense thriller, while also being one of the funniest K-dramas out there. Crash Landing on You has something for everyone.

Even viewers who have never watched a K-drama will find Se-ri and Jeong-hyeok's dynamic endearing because of how well-written it is. The two have lived completely different lives, but they slowly realize they understand each other better than anyone else ever has. The border itself is what gives the romance its ache, since the two come from countries that can never truly let them be together. Every loving moment they share feels borrowed, and the audience spends the whole series dreading the day all this ends. Crash Landing on You is the perfect gateway into K-dramas, and it's easily one of the most beloved romances of the decade.

In Pride and Prejudice, Mary Bennet is mostly remembered for embarrassing herself at the piano while the rest of the room cringes. The Other Bennet Sister, based on Janice Hadlow's novel, finally gives her a story of her own. Mary (Ella Bruccoleri) is the bookish, awkward middle child who has spent her life being compared to her prettier and livelier sisters. When she gets the chance to start fresh in London with her kind aunt and uncle, the Gardiners (Indira Varma and Richard Coyle), Mary finally gets to discover who she is outside of her family's shadow. In London, she finds herself caught between two very different suitors, the reserved Mr. Hayward (Dónal Finn) and the charming Mr. Ryder (Laurie Davidson). Now, for a woman who has never been anyone's first choice, being noticed is thrilling and terrifying at once.

The love triangle isn't really about which man wins her over, though. It's about Mary learning what she actually wants, rather than settling for whoever will have her. The series turns one of Austen's punchlines into a heroine worth rooting for, and that's exactly why it's so satisfying. Mary is prickly and earnest, and she's funnier than anyone ever gave her credit for. Her love story doesn't arrive in one sweeping moment. Instead, it grows alongside her confidence, which makes every step toward happiness feel truly earned. Anyone who has ever felt like the overlooked one in their family will see a little of themselves in her. The Other Bennet Sister is a sweet, clever twist on Austen, and it's the kind of underdog romance that's impossible not to love.

Avengers: Doomsday Billboard May Have Spoiled Thor's Biggest Power Upgrade Yet

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Doomsday is almost upon us, and no, I'm not talking about Joe Russo's wretched vision of an AI garbage future. I am, of course, talking about he and his brother's latest Marvel team-up movie "Avengers: Doomsday" which may have just been partially spoiled by, of all thing, a billboard.

Per a Twitter/X post from Marvel Updates, new "Doomsday" billboards have gone up in Shanghai, one of which depicts Robert Downey Jr.'s Victor Von Doom summoning an energy blast. The other, however, is a bit more interesting. It shows Florence Pugh's Yelena Belova/Black Widow, Chris Evans' Steve Rogers/ ex-Captain America, Chris Hemsworth's Thor, and James Marsden's Scott Summers/Cyclops similarly wielding their various powers. But some fans have pointed out that the lightning surrounding Thor — who's seen holding his battle axe, Stormbreaker — appears yellow as opposed to the usual blue hue used in previous films and the "Doomsday" trailers themselves.

New banners for 'AVENGERS: DOOMSDAY' have been spotted in Shanghai. pic.twitter.com/iCOsrgdumJ

— Marvel Updates (@marvel_updat3s) November 10, 2026

This is notable, because it suggests Thor could be using a hugely powerful force known as the Odinforce in "Doomsday." This power, previously unused by the character in the Marvel Cinematic Universe, was created by the Kings of Asgard, and it could prove pretty handy in the Avengers' upcoming clash with Doctor Doom. For now, it remains unclear whether this billboard actually does spoil the inclusion of Odinforce in "Doomsday," but it would make a lot of sense for Thor to power up ahead of his battle with RDJ's villain. The only thing is, there's a major trade-off that comes with using this particular power, which could mean Hemsworth's hero is about to take a long nap.

The Odinforce comes with a big caveat. Those who use it must enter what's known as Odinsleep, whereby they become unconscious while recharging. If the Shanghai billboard does, in fact, show Thor using the Odinforce, that could spark a dramatic storyline for the character.

For some reason, Chris Hemsworth's hero has been given the character arc of Ross from "Friends." He started out as a fearless and formidable leader before demonstrating a more humorous side and eventually becoming a bit of a joke himself. 2019's "Avengers: Endgame" in particular was accused of fat-shaming the grief-stricken Thor, concluding the God of Thunder's journey from serious warrior to silly superhero. Even Hemsworth told GQ that 2022's "Thor: Love and Thunder" was "too silly," adding, "I think we just had too much fun."

When the remarkably dull "Avengers: Doomsday" teaser trailers debuted in early 2026, we did at least get our first look at a much more serious Thor, who will clearly be a lot less "silly" in the upcoming team-up movie than he has in other recent MCU projects. All of which is to say that allowing him to wield the Odinforce in "Doomsday," which could involve a major sacrifice, would be a nice way to treat the character more seriously, providing a dramatic storyline that perhaps sees him willingly embrace Odinsleep in his attempt to overpower Doctor Doom and save his friends.

As unimportant as it may seem to the uninitiated, the appearance of heroes' powers in "Avengers: Doomsday" is clearly something to which Marvel Studios pays close attention. We've already seen the studio upgrade the look of Doctor Doom's power energy to more closely resemble the famous Kirby Krackle of the comic books. As such, the color of Thor's lightning isn't as trivial at it may seem.

This isn't technically the first time fans have spotted hints of the Odinforce in "Avengers: Doomsday" promotional material either. Back in July 2026, Earth 616 posted on Twitter/X highlighting promotional art in which Thor is shown holding Stormbreaker with orange/yellow lightning surrounding the weapon.

Thor's lightning in the MCU has always been blue-white pure electrical power channeled through Mjolnir or Stormbreaker.

The yellow-gold lightning in this promotional poster is completely different from anything we have seen before.

In the comics the Odinforce manifests as... pic.twitter.com/4JQKlPte14

— Earth 616 (@MarvelExrth616) July 12, 2026

The post also points out that Thor prays to Odin in his "Doomsday" teaser trailer, saying, "Lend to me the strength of the All-Fathers, so that I might fight once more." All of this combined with the latest billboard revelation suggests we'll be seeing some sort of upgraded Thor in "Doomsday," though at this point, none of the speculation has been confirmed by Marvel.

Whatever happens, it seems the Russo Brothers have a much more serious story planned for Thor in "Doomsday," which is good news. Trailers have shown the character trying and failing to attack Doctor Doom and delivering solemn speeches to the various heroes who gather together to take on this latest threat to the MCU. Gaining the power of the Odinforce would be an exciting development that allows Hemsworth's character to finally regain his standing as one of Marvel's most formidable heroes.

"Avengers: Doomsday" hits theaters on December 18, 2026.

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Chip designer AMD gained as investors weighed its AI deal push, with trading well above normal, today, Nov. 10, 2026.

Advanced Micro Devices (AMD +1.30%), a data-center and PC chip designer, closed at $515.21, up 1.30%. Tuesday's gains followed a premarket upgrade and fresh AI-chip deal chatter, and investors are watching data-center demand and AI spending next.

Trading volume reached 46.7 million shares, coming in about 102% above its three-month average of 23.2 million shares.

The S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.38%) closed at 7,521, down 0.38%, while the Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC -0.74%) ended at 25,066, down 0.74%. Among semiconductors, x86 CPUs, GPUs, AI accelerators, and data-center/server chip stocks, Nvidia (NVDA -5.47%) fell 5.47% to $168.89 and Intel (INTC -2.09%) dropped 2.09% to $58.13, while AMD outperformed both chip rivals.

AMD stock rose today after artificial intelligence giant Anthropic disclosed in an amended IPO filing on Nov. 10 that AMD would be part of a group involved in a $26 billion private placement alongside some of the world's biggest technology companies. AMD plans to invest $2.5 billion worth of non-voting Class N common stock in Anthropic, which counts toward an earlier commitment of up to $5 billion linked to chip deliveries.

AMD shares have been on a run this week after its chief rival, Nvidia, disclosed an investigation into its CEO, Jensen Huang, over his relationship with an alleged Chinese spy. Wall Street's fears that Huang could be forced to step down caused investors to shift into AMD stock.

This added to analyst upgrades of the company's shares, as its business continues to boom. AMD's Nov. 3 earnings results showed record quarterly revenue of $13.4 billion and Q4 revenue guidance of about $17 billion, which kept AI-driven growth in focus.

I have long been an admirer of AMD's CEO, Lisa Su, which is one of the factors behind my buying shares. The company's performance demonstrates her skill at leading the semiconductor giant as it capitalizes on massive AI demand.

Germany U21 ‘television tower’ keeper Tom Ritzy Hülsmann leaves teammates looking up

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Germany U21’s newest goalkeeper Tom Ritzy Hülsmann has already made quite an impression on his international teammates. The 22-year-old Stade Reims shot-stopper stands an imposing 2.05 metres tall, and his first encounters with fellow internationals Muhammed Damar and Maurice Krattenmacher have provided some entertaining stories.

Speaking at Tuesday’s DFB U21 press conference, attended remotely by Get German Football News, the two midfielders offered amusing accounts of their introductions to the towering goalkeeper. Hülsmann, a former Bayern Munich youth prospect, received his first call-up to any German national team when head coach Antonio Di Salvo announced his squad for November’s internationals against Belgium and Croatia.

The Trier native spent last season with Austrian Bundesliga outfit TSV Hartberg, where he was voted the league’s best goalkeeper. His performances earned him a summer transfer to French second-division side Stade Reims. Hülsmann’s impressive physical stature was the subject of some lighthearted discussion as Tuesday’s press conference drew to a close.

DFB U21 press spokesperson Markus Beer brought up the subject of Germany’s towering new goalkeeper, noting that his arrival had apparently produced some rather unusual situations when the players first greeted him.

“He’s like a television tower,” the Wolfsburg midfielder explained. “When I first saw him, I asked him what tablets he takes. He told me it’s just genetics. Apparently, his parents are very tall too.”

The 22-year-old continued with an amusing account of their initial handshake.

“When he stood in front of me and wanted to shake my hand, I had to look up three storeys,” Damar said. “I thought [Joshua] Quarshie was already tall, but compared to him, he’s an ant.”

Damar’s reference was to fellow Germany U21 defender Joshua Quarshie, another player who possesses an imposing physical stature.

Elversberg midfielder Maurice Krattenmacher, who earned a late call-up to the squad following several injury-related withdrawals, was similarly amused by his first encounter with Hülsmann.

“I can only agree,” Krattenmacher said. “You’ve got a huge tower coming towards you. But he’s a great guy, and I’m looking forward to training with him.”

Beer acknowledged that Hülsmann’s height had indeed produced some peculiar moments during the team’s initial introductions, bringing the press conference to an entertaining conclusion.

The amusing exchange provided some levity at the end of a press conference otherwise focused on Germany’s preparations for next summer’s European U21 Championship.

Earlier in proceedings, Beer had briefly intervened to emphasize that the upcoming encounters with Belgium and Croatia should be regarded as preparation matches for the European Championship rather than simply friendlies.

Germany have already secured qualification for next summer’s tournament in Albania and Serbia, with the November internationals providing Di Salvo with an opportunity to integrate new players and evaluate his team’s progress against fellow European Championship participants.

The Germans host Belgium at the Stadion an der Hafenstraße in Essen on Friday, November 13, before travelling to Rijeka to face Croatia four days later.

Hülsmann will be hoping to make his first international appearance during the upcoming fixtures. Whatever happens on the pitch, the Stade Reims goalkeeper has certainly already made his presence felt among his new teammates.
